# Donald Trump

Donald Trump appears in the article as the counterexample to the Obama-era battlefield restrictions.[[1]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-dereliction-of-duty)

Posing the question of how Trump destroyed [[isis|ISIS]] so quickly, and citing Q drop 1887 of August 15, 2018, the article states that Trump immediately removed the [[white-house|White House]] from the chain of command on the battlefield.[[1]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-dereliction-of-duty) With what it calls the handcuffs removed, the military was unleashed and destroyed ISIS in less than a year.[[1]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-dereliction-of-duty) This is set directly against the practice attributed to [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]], under which every military action required White House approval — an arrangement the article equates with a [[stand-down-orders|stand down order]] protecting ISIS and Al Qaeda.[[1]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-dereliction-of-duty)

## Hollywood and cultural strategy

Trump appointed [[jon-voight|Jon Voight]], [[sylvester-stallone|Sylvester Stallone]] and [[mel-gibson|Mel Gibson]] as ambassadors to [[hollywood|Hollywood]], a move [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] reads as proof that Trump considers the industry salvageable rather than something to burn to the ground.[[2]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-new-hollywood) Carlson argues Trump understands Hollywood could again be used to inspire and shape audiences while entertaining them, and calls him "Reagan 2.0" — a vastly improved version of [[ronald-reagan|Ronald Reagan]]'s approach that does not include compromising with the enemy.[[2]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-new-hollywood)

Trump sued [[cbs|CBS]] for $10 billion over the edited [[60-minutes|60 Minutes]] interview with [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]], which he viewed as election tampering; the suit was dismissed as frivolous before CBS parent [[paramount-global|Paramount Global]] began discussing a settlement while pursuing its merger with [[skydance-media|Skydance Media]].[[2]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-new-hollywood) Carlson concludes that Hollywood has already been infiltrated by Trump's people and is being used to prepare the public for his agenda, including taking down the [[mexican-drug-cartel|Mexican drug cartel]] and reforming rather than scattering the [[cia|CIA]].[[2]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-new-hollywood)

## Technology agenda

Trump rolled out [[project-stargate|Project Stargate]], a plan for $500 billion of US investment in artificial intelligence infrastructure in partnership with [[oracle|Oracle]], [[openai|OpenAI]] and [[softbank|SoftBank]], and suggested that either [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] or [[larry-ellison|Larry Ellison]] buy [[tiktok|TikTok]].[[2]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-new-hollywood) Carlson notes that critics oscillate between treating Trump as a genius and as mentally challenged, a superhero and a super villain, with some becoming unhinged within two days of his taking office.[[2]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-new-hollywood)

## Threat to the flow of money

"A Conflict of Interest" opens by asking why the enemy is desperately trying to kill President Trump, and answers that anyone who tries to stop the flow of money from taxpayers to the hidden enemy and their puppets is deemed a threat — and that when such a person holds the most powerful office in the world and commands the most feared fighting force in the world, extreme measures are used to eliminate that threat.[[3]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-conflict-of-interest) Trump and his family are described as risking their lives daily in a war Trump accepted as real long before announcing his candidacy.[[3]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-conflict-of-interest) The struggle is framed as one between insiders and outsiders, with Trump as the outsiders' champion who took up the mantle of his close friend [[jfk-jr|JFK Jr.]], whose death the article attributes to the same enemy.[[3]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-conflict-of-interest)

## Actions against the network

Because he was an outsider and a threat to the cabal's control, the article says, Trump ended the wars, closing a twenty-year conflict in Afghanistan it argues would otherwise have run another twenty and produced further trillions in [[taxpayer-theft|taxpayer theft]].[[3]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-conflict-of-interest) With Trump's approval, Saudi Arabia and other Arab states blockaded [[qatar|Qatar]] during the [[qatar-diplomatic-crisis-2017|2017 diplomatic crisis]] to cut off one of the biggest funding sources for [[al-qaeda|Al-Qaeda]]; [[abdelhakim-belhaj|Abdelhakim Belhaj]] was promptly watchlisted and, in January 2019, made the subject of a Libyan warrant for abuses and war crimes, driving him to Istanbul.[[3]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-conflict-of-interest) The article states that Trump has never forgotten [[benghazi|Benghazi]] and that payback is coming.[[3]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-conflict-of-interest)

## On the auto industry (2023)

During the 2023 [[summer-of-strikes-2023|Summer of Strikes]], Trump said in a speech that "the auto industry is being assassinated," a judgment Badlands Media endorsed as 100% correct given the optimization policies that streamlined and automated manufacturing processes and the global sourcing policies that moved remaining jobs outside the United States.[[4]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-time-to-strike) His remark is set against President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s appearance on the [[united-auto-workers|UAW]] picket line, which the same article calls astonishing in light of an economic policy it holds responsible for destroying US manufacturing.[[4]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-time-to-strike)

## Banking and postal agenda

"A Seismic Shift" argues that Trump is quietly restructuring the American financial system to transfer power from private banks back to the people.[[5]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-seismic-shift) It credits him with four initiatives: a move back to the [[gold-standard|gold standard]], a behind-the-scenes push for a digital dollar, a Postal Banking [[public-banking-option|public option]], and the creation of [[fedaccounts|FedAccounts]].[[5]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-seismic-shift) The article states that Trump took control of the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] by appointing every member of the board of governors except one — a governor said to have been retained because she supported the digital dollar, as do Jared Kushner and Trump himself — and that this was part of a plan to take down the global debt system.[[5]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-seismic-shift)

Trump is said to have done the same at the [[united-states-postal-service|Post Office]]. When he took office in January 2017 the [[usps-board-of-governors|USPS Board of Governors]] was entirely empty after years without a quorum, giving him the opportunity to appoint the whole nine-member body; that board later chose [[louis-dejoy|Louis DeJoy]] as Postmaster General.[[5]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-seismic-shift) The Washington Post described Trump as having fixated on the Postal Service soon after taking office and as having reshaped its once-obscure governing board into a behind-the-scenes powerhouse setting his priorities in motion for years to come.[[5]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-seismic-shift) Badlands Media argues Trump intends to make the Post Office "great again" and use it both to protect elections — via a USPS blockchain voting patent it reads as the foundation of citizen voter ID and cleansed voter rolls — and to transform the banking system through a revived [[postal-savings-system|Postal Savings System]].[[5]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-seismic-shift) The author closes by stating his belief that Trump is a hero focused on the people's best interests and deserves their trust.[[5]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-seismic-shift)

## 2024 candidacy announcement (November 2022)

Trump officially announced his candidacy for the 2024 presidential election in a November 2022 address at [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]], days after the [[2022-midterm-elections|2022 midterm elections]].[[6]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/all-assets-deployed) Badlands Media had anticipated the announcement's function in advance: a dual opportunity to separate the MAGA and [[america-first-movement|America First]] wheat from the [[con-inc|Con Inc.]] controlled-opposition chaff, while undercutting political moves by other influential figures gathering directed political energy from the conservative base in an attempt to sweep it out from under Trump's leadership and stewardship.[[6]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/all-assets-deployed) [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]] and other close allies had projected that the announcement would light the media world on fire.[[6]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/all-assets-deployed)

The speech contained no major revelations, but Badlands highlighted Trump's statement that "the voting will be much, much different in 2024" and his warning that things would get "much worse" before people "see clearly" — remarks read as consistent with long-standing precipice theories and with a [[continuity-of-government|Continuity of Government]] framing.[[6]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/all-assets-deployed) Trump suggested that the reachable middle of the public was not yet ready to turn fully on the globalist interests and the System of Systems used, in the article's terms, to subjugate rather than lead.[[6]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/all-assets-deployed)

## Narrative strategy

Badlands commentary describes Trump as a professed and ardent student of Sun Tzu, invoking the maxim that one must appear weak when strong and then fall like a thunderbolt when the enemy least expects it.[[6]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/all-assets-deployed) On this reading, expectations that Trump would audit the midterms on stage or announce the arrest of the sitting president misread both the venue and the strategy, and the apparent setbacks of November 2022 did not fundamentally alter the movement's trajectory.[[6]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/all-assets-deployed)

## Tax policy and the July 2024 crisis

On June 10, 2024, Trump first publicly proposed that, as president, he would work toward making tips tax-free.[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember) Badlands commentary reads the [[tax-free-tips|tax-free tips]] pledge not as pandering to service workers for votes but as a deliberate first step toward reducing and eventually abolishing the [[federal-income-tax|federal income tax]], after which the money supply system itself would be the next target — a prospect the [[deep-state-cabal|Deep State Cabal]] could not survive.[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember) [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argues that this subtle announcement, roughly two weeks before the 2024 presidential debate, is the likeliest trigger for the Cabal's sudden desperation, rather than public recognition of [[joe-biden|Biden]]'s cognitive decline.[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember) Carlson expects Trump to fund government through tariffs and corporate taxes while shrinking spending, on the principle of "less government, less government control."[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember)

Three days after the Butler rally shooting cycle began — the [[attempted-assassination-of-donald-trump-2024|attempted assassination of July 13, 2024]] — Trump named [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] as his running mate, the Republican National Convention proceeded, and Biden exited the race.[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember)

## Monetary record

Carlson credits Trump with forcing the first post-2008 interest rate normalization: the initial hike followed his 2016 election win, he was obliged to retain [[janet-yellen|Janet Yellen]] as Fed Chair for two years, and in 2018 his appointee [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] raised rates aggressively — producing a 20% market drop that autumn and cuts in 2019.[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember) Trump, in this account, knew rates had to rise but insisted the process be gradual.[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember) When the Covid lockdowns arrived in early 2020 he forced the [[federal-reserve|Fed]] to print money at an unprecedented rate, knowingly creating massive [[inflation|inflation]] as the lesser of two evils against a Cabal plan to destroy the American economy — "fighting the Cabal with their own weapon."[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember) Carlson also recalls Trump telling Amazon to deliver its own packages rather than be subsidized through the [[united-states-postal-service|USPS]], and notes that Trump met with the leaders of the [[brics|BRICS]] nations in 2017.[[7]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-week-to-remember)

## 2016 election and the legitimacy campaign

Trump's unexpected defeat of Secretary of State [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] on election night in 2016 triggered a sustained campaign against the legitimacy of his presidency.[[8]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/always-fight-back) Democrats claimed he had "colluded" with Russia to steal the election and launched the Mueller investigation, which cost taxpayers millions and found no evidence of collusion, while referring to him as illegitimate throughout his term.[[8]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/always-fight-back) On Inauguration Day, January 20, 2017, [[free-speech-for-people|Free Speech For People]] launched a campaign to impeach him, announced in a *Washington Post* article titled "The Campaign to Impeach President Trump Has Begun."[[8]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/always-fight-back)

In later [[lawfare]] against his supporters — notably [[naacp-v-useip|*NAACP et al. v. USEIP et al.*]] — plaintiffs advanced the theory that Trump supporters questioning election outcomes or knocking on doors constituted voter intimidation, a position defendants argued becomes a problem only when it involves 2020 and Trump.[[8]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/always-fight-back)

## Reviving the Bretton Woods institutions

In "A Gateway to a New World of Cooperation" (3 September 2025), [[matthew-ehret|Matthew Ehret]] names Trump — alongside [[john-f-kennedy|John F. Kennedy]], though to a lesser if in his view still relevant extent — as a figure who attempted to revive the noble origins of the [[bretton-woods-system|Bretton Woods]] institutions, including the [[imf|IMF]] and the [[world-bank|World Bank]], after their post-war capture by deep-state operatives.[[9]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-gateway-to-a-new-world-of-cooperation)

## Covid communications and the "virus" metaphor (2023 analysis)

In the July 17, 2023 Badlands Media article *A Virus, or a Metaphor?*, [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argued that Trump's public statements are deliberately layered messages aimed at several audiences at once, a practice described on the [[trump-comms|Trump comms]] page.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor) Carlson wrote that most people in MAGA agree Trump is a very smart man whose messages can be confusing, and who probably loves his country more than any other politician alive.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor)

Carlson addressed the most prominent reason former supporters abandoned Trump: the claim that he told people to get vaccinated while knowing the [[covid-19-vaccines|Covid vaccines]] were dangerous.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor) His answer was that Trump knew the plan of the Cabal was to keep the world locked down for as long as five years in order to destroy the middle class and create a two-tier society, and that Trump therefore forced [[big-pharma|Big Pharma]] to accelerate vaccine development as the only way to end the [[covid-19-lockdowns|lockdowns]] before permanent damage was done.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor) Carlson emphasized that Trump alone among government and corporate leaders insisted vaccines be a choice and not a requirement, which he read as the decoding key: had Trump believed them safe and effective he would have mandated them.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor)

On the substance of the messaging, Carlson held that Trump places a double meaning on the word "virus," using it to signify Communism — a contrived plan "created in a lab and dropped on us" to destroy America from within, paralleling Trump's own claim that Covid was intentionally created in a lab and released on the world.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor) The repeated reference to the [[spanish-flu|Spanish Flu]] "of 1917," for which Trump was mocked because the pandemic occurred in 1918, was in this reading a pointer to the October 1917 [[bolshevik-revolution|Bolshevik uprising]].[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor) Likewise Trump's insistence on the "Chyna virus," for which he was called racist, was read as a statement that the [[chinese-communist-party|CCP]] is infiltrating America.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor)

Carlson also cited Trump's material position as evidence against the claim that he belongs to the [[deep-state-cabal|Deep State Cabal]]: his net worth declined after becoming president, the [[securities-and-exchange-commission|SEC]] held up the DWAC–[[trump-media-and-technology-group|Trump Media]] merger and thereby capital for [[truth-social|Truth Social]], and the mainstream media demonizes him uniformly.[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor) In the same article Carlson noted that, on the [[qanon|Q]] reading of Tom Clancy's *[[the-sum-of-all-fears|The Sum of All Fears]]*, the two men the modern-day Nazis have never been able to control are Trump and [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]].[[10]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/a-virus-or-a-metaphor)

## Draining the swamp (2024 commentary)

Writing in April 2024, [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] named Trump as the man capable of administering what the Joker in the 1989 *Batman* film called an enema for the town — draining the swamp, exterminating "the rats that live in the sewer system and the cockroaches that live between the walls."[[11]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/americas-greatest-threat) He cautioned that unless Trump removes all of the cancer, no permanent change will occur.[[11]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/americas-greatest-threat)

Carlson argued that Trump taking office in 2025 would mark the beginning of the real work rather than a finish line: the point at which "the hoarder's trash will begin to be thrown out."[[11]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/americas-greatest-threat) Continuing the metaphor, he warned that the hoarder must also be cured of the underlying disorder, or the house will simply fill up again — a condition he tied to a complete overhaul of the American political system, the removal of criminals from power, and the installation of failsafes so that the [[us-constitution|Constitution]] holds authority over the government.[[11]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/americas-greatest-threat)

## Criminal indictments over the 2020 election

Trump was indicted in 2023 both federally by special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] and at the state level in Fulton County, Georgia by [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]].[[12]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/are-jack-smith-and-fani-willis-in) Badlands commentary holds that both indictments rest fundamentally on the allegation that Trump lied about the 2020 election — that he secretly knew he had lost — making his alleged state of mind the crime from which all other charged offenses flow.[[12]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/are-jack-smith-and-fani-willis-in) Willis's indictment carries 41 counts, including a RICO count listing 161 predicate acts; Smith's carries four counts, each requiring bad faith.[[12]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/are-jack-smith-and-fani-willis-in)

The article argues the prosecutions may backfire, because as a criminal defendant Trump acquires discovery powers and a due-process right to prove his statements true, allowing [[2020-election-fraud-claims|election-fraud evidence]] onto a court record for the first time after 50 of 55 prior cases were dismissed on [[standing-and-jurisdiction|standing]].[[12]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/are-jack-smith-and-fani-willis-in) It further notes that Trump has never contradicted himself on the 2020 election, complicating any attempt to prove him a liar.[[12]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/are-jack-smith-and-fani-willis-in)

Beyond the legal front, the piece contends the indictments improve Trump's optics: despite being a billionaire, he now has a mugshot and faces the same broken system many Americans feel persecuted by, building the popular support threshold the author deems necessary for the next phase of the [[fifth-generation-warfare|5G war]].[[12]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/are-jack-smith-and-fani-willis-in) The trial motif is also read as the fulfillment of the [[white-squall|White Squall]] comm within [[qanon|Q]] lore.[[12]](https://badlands.substack.com/p/are-jack-smith-and-fani-willis-in)

## CBDCs and the financial plan

Joe Lange's June 2023 article "Are CBDCs The Path To Fear, or Freedom?" casts Trump as the director of the transition out of the [[debt-slavery|global debt slavery system]].[13] Lange argues Trump took control of the most dominant nation in the world both militarily and financially, controls the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] and the dollar, and is using them to bankrupt the global debt system — since a system must collapse before it can be replaced.[13] Control of the Treasury and the Fed is attributed to [[devolution|devolution]] and [[continuity-of-government|Continuity of Government]] plans already in place.[13]

Lange notes that Trump, unlike right-wing media, governors, and members of [[congress|Congress]], has never attacked [[central-bank-digital-currency|CBDCs]], and reads that silence as deliberate: blockchain settlement is the tool needed to move the country quickly into a new gold-backed system when the dollar crashes, limiting the duration of the suffering.[13] He identifies [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]] as the driving force behind the Fed CBDC push and points to Trump's 2017 elevation of [[j-christopher-giancarlo|J. Christopher Giancarlo]] to chair the [[commodity-futures-trading-commission|CFTC]] as an early move in the same plan.[13]

Finally, Lange contends Trump prioritized flipping the courts in order to set precedent, engineering a fight between the Federal Reserve and the states that would bring the Legal Tender Cases, the Gold Clause decisions, and the [[federal-reserve-act-of-1913|Federal Reserve Act of 1913]] before the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] — the aim being a redeemable, [[gold-standard|gold-backed]] digital dollar that protects constitutional rights.[13]

## Iran and the factional war

[[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] presents Trump's rise as inseparable from the fight over the [[joint-comprehensive-plan-of-action|Iran Nuclear Deal]].[14] The Saudi–Israeli coalition born of the Obama [[state-department|State Department]]'s Iranian power play is described as one of the major catalysts creating the conditions and support necessary for Trump to rise to the top of the Republican Party and compete with the Clinton machine, with [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]] backing what amounted to regime change in the United States.[14] DeLarme's blunt formulation is that "no Israel, no Trump 1 or 2."[14]

On taking office Trump reversed the working foreign policy consensus from maximum pressure on Russia to [[maximum-pressure-campaign|maximum pressure]] on [[iran|Iran]] and [[communist-china|China]], a maneuver DeLarme says "perhaps more than anything else, put the crosshairs on Trump's back."[14] Withdrawing from the deal, he threatened every country on Earth with "enormous consequences" for evading Iran sanctions; [[tom-donilon|Tom Donilon]] called the withdrawal "the worst move in the Middle East since the Iraq War."[14] Trump's relationship with [[narendra-modi|Modi]] is described as fantastic and growing, with Trump seeking to build up India as a wedge against China.[14]

DeLarme argues the Iran question had to be handled with care: mishandled, it could have rendered Trump a lame duck by fracturing his base so badly that Republicans were wrecked in the midterms, yet it could not be ignored entirely.[14] Whether Trump can hold his coalition together while countering the [[foreign-policy-establishment|foreign policy establishment]]'s entrenched power will determine whether he reshapes the global order or falls victim to its traps.[14]

## Infrastructure and great-power cooperation

[[matthew-ehret|Matthew Ehret]] credits Trump with making an honest endeavour to restore a sane security doctrine by focusing American interests on healing from more than fifty years of self-inflicted atrophy under globalized outsourcing, militarism and post-industrialism, despite constant sabotage by the [[deep-state|Deep State]] and an unusually large and independent military-intelligence industrial complex.[15] In April 2019 Trump said that between Russia, China and the United States "we're all making hundreds of billions of dollars' worth of weapons, including nuclear, which is ridiculous," and proposed that the three countries come together to stop the spending and redirect it toward things more productive for long-term peace.[15]

This call for a U.S.-Russia-China cooperative policy ran in tandem with the first phase of the U.S.-China trade deal that took effect in January 2020, guaranteeing $350 billion of U.S. finished goods purchased by China; Ehret notes that [[george-soros|George Soros]] suffered a public meltdown that same month, naming Trump's U.S. and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]]'s China as the two greatest threats to his global open society.[15]

On September 26, 2020, Trump announced in a tweet that federal support would go to the [[alaska-canada-railway|Alaska-Canada Railway]], a 2,570 km line connecting Alaska for the first time with Canada and the lower 48 states, with the Alaska-Alberta Railway Development Corporation tasked with building and managing the $17 billion project.[15]

## Financial system signalling and the 2024 Bitcoin Conference

[[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] argued that Trump hinted to the [[deep-state|Deep State]] that he would tear down the walls of the financial system to its foundation on returning to the White House, and that he conveyed this by beginning to talk about [[tax-free-tips|tax-free tips]] — a chain leading from a capped income tax to abolition of the [[federal-income-tax|income tax]] to the destruction of the financial system as presently known.[16] Trump allowed the financial system to remain intact during his first presidency because the timing was not right for what he planned to begin in 2025.[16]

While the [[paris-olympics-2024-opening-ceremony|Olympic opening ceremony]] controversy consumed public attention, Trump was speaking at the [[bitcoin|Bitcoin]] Conference, where he said he saw Bitcoin as an important part of the American economy going forward and that he wanted the US Government to stockpile it — not as a replacement for the dollar but as a means of strengthening it, from which every American would benefit.[16] Carlson also pointed to Trump's selection of [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], with Vance's Big Tech and cryptocurrency connections, as a clue to the same agenda, and noted Trump's statement that the economy will flourish in his next administration though not by leaving the same people in control of the financial markets.[16] The advice offered to readers is to listen to what Trump says about the economy, since the subtle things he says under his breath are what really matter.[16]

Carlson further asserted that Trump's people already know exactly who was responsible for the [[attempted-assassination-of-donald-trump-2024|assassination attempt]] and who controls them, that those responsible will not go unpunished, and that if Trump died the patriots would continue, there being a contingency plan in place.[16]

## Commentary on the Ayatollah reports (March 2026)

In the March 28, 2026 twelfth episode of [[the-blitz|The Blitz]], Trump features in the dueling narratives over the reported death of the Iranian Ayatollah, which the episode's hosts characterise as "fake and gay, but also real and possibly not gay" — adding that according to Trump the Ayatollah is "actually probably gay, according to what he's been told."[17] The hosts used Trump's second-hand framing of the story as a jumping-off point for a discussion of [[narrative-warfare|narrative warfare]], [[sovereign-disentanglement|sovereign disentanglement]] and [[first-principles|first principles]].[17]

## Big Tech and the 2025 inauguration

In "America Has a CIA-Run Economy," [[erik-carlson|Erik Carlson]] observed that many chief executives, especially in big tech, had endorsed Trump or at least stopped directly working against him, with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] the most supportive vocally and financially, [[mark-zuckerberg|Mark Zuckerberg]] opening Meta's platforms to more free speech and terminating DEI programs, and [[jeff-bezos|Jeff Bezos]] pledging that the *[[washington-post|Washington Post]]* would be less biased and more trustworthy.[18] Carlson read the million-dollar [[trump-inaugural-fund-donations|inaugural fund donations]] from Google, [[microsoft|Microsoft]], [[meta-platforms|Meta]], [[amazon|Amazon]], [[apple|Apple]] and [[openai|OpenAI]] as "the real pardoning that is happening in America," with Trump allowing companies valuable to the economy to essentially kiss the ring — while noting that [[nvidia|Nvidia]] was conspicuously absent from the donor list.[18]

Carlson concluded that Trump regards these firms as a necessary part of the American economy, since it is hard to imagine an America without Amazon, Apple, Facebook, Microsoft or Google, and that Trump the businessman understands the economy cannot be destroyed in order to obtain the justice patriots believe is deserved.[18] He predicted nonetheless that under Trump some mega-corporations will be broken up into smaller companies, that [[cia|CIA]] and Deep State strings will continue to be cut, and that American innovation will increasingly come from America-born citizens.[18]

## Syria policy and the fall of Assad

Badlands Media noted that the termination of the [[cia|CIA]]'s [[timber-sycamore|Timber Sycamore]] program in 2017 under President Trump was seen as a shift in U.S. policy, focusing more explicitly on counter-terrorism rather than regime change in [[syria|Syria]].[19]

Writing as the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration prepared to transfer power to "Team Trump" after the December 2024 collapse of [[bashar-al-assad|Bashar al-Assad]]'s government, the article expressed hope that the United States would stay out of the situation and avoid another multimillion-dollar aid package bolstering rebel forces for a future conflict.[19] It described the Trump policy — often labeled derogatorily as "noninterventionist" — as the ideal disposition toward the conflict, arguing that one of the many reasons for Trump's victory in November 2024 was that the American people are sick of funding conflicts abroad.[19] The article also discussed [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]], Trump's pick for Director of National Intelligence, whose 2017 meeting with Assad was revived by critics casting her as a Putin puppet.[19]

The eleventh episode of [[the-blitz|The Blitz]], published 21 March 2026, devoted part of its discussion to Trump and what its hosts called the dangers of playing white hat–black hat games with the characters that orbit him on the [[fifth-generation-warfare|fifth-generation]] game board.[20] Hosts [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] and [[ghost-of-patrick-henry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] raised the point alongside their reading of the [[iran|Iran]] War and [[nato|NATO]] narrative as a pincer by the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] against the [[invisible-enemy|Invisible Enemy]], cautioning against confident assignment of allegiance to figures in Trump's orbit.[20]

Episode 14 of [[the-blitz|The Blitz]], published 25 April 2026, devoted a segment to what the "[[taco-tuesday|TACO Tuesday]]" crowd gets wrong about Trump, treating the assumption that he reliably backs down from his stated positions as a persistent analytical error among market commentators.[22] The same episode argued that there might be much more to Trump's overtures toward [[spirit-airlines|Spirit Airlines]] than meets the eye, presenting the airline story as a signal rather than a routine commercial item.[22] Both segments sat inside an episode framed around institutional accountability, alongside the indictment of the [[southern-poverty-law-center|Southern Poverty Law Center]] and the unraveling of the [[russiagate-hoax|Russiagate Hoax]] — the latter a matter bearing directly on the investigations of Trump's 2016 campaign.[22]

## Prospective alignment with Xi Jinping (May 2026)

The seventeenth episode of [[the-blitz|The Blitz]], published 16 May 2026 under the title "Big Trump Visits Little China as Election Deniers Seize the Narrative," devoted much of its running time to a prospective Trump–[[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] alliance.[23] Hosts [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] and [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued that although this pairing attracts less commentary than possible coordination between Trump and [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], it may in fact be the alignment the globalist enemy fears most.[23] The same episode paired the discussion with the shifting American narrative on [[election-denial|election denial]], which the hosts described as more popular and more bipartisan than ever before.[23]

## World tour and NATO (July 2026)

Episode 24 of [[the-blitz|The Blitz]], "The Trump Tornado Touches Down at NATO," published 11 July 2026, was devoted to what its hosts called Trump's latest "super rapid" world tour.[24] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] and [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued that the tour represented a total inversion of everything the System had taught the public about the Axis and the Allies, with his arrival at [[nato|NATO]] as its emblematic moment.[24] They held that the System's operators had ended up believing their own narrative, and that Trump was untangling that narrative in real time — and doing so with some unlikely friends.[24]

## Generational divide (July 2026)

Episode 23 of [[the-blitz|The Blitz]], "Generation America," published 3 July 2026, devoted a frank and direct segment to the [[generational-divide|generational divide]] that hosts [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] and [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] said Trump is being forced to navigate between Boomers, Millennials and Gen Z.[25] The same episode examined the intra-party civil wars afflicting both the Right and Left sides of the [[uniparty|Uniparty]], the political backdrop against which the hosts placed Trump's generational balancing act.[25]

And Who's Fallen Into It," was built around what its hosts termed Trump's "glorious discombobulator" — the disorienting effect his manoeuvres exert on allies, opponents and commentators alike — and around the question of who had fallen into the trap it set.[26] Hosts [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] and [[ghost-of-patrick-henry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] described the effects of that discombobulation as ongoing and amplified rather than spent.[26] The episode connected the phenomenon to the real reason for the perpetuation of the [[fake-iran-war|Fake Iran War]] and to the construction of the [[multipolar-world|Multipolar World]], which the hosts said is being built in plain sight for those who cultivate the eyes to see it.[26]

## Alien Disclosure Op (February 2026)

Episode 7 of [[badlands-blitz|The Blitz]], published February 21, 2026, characterised a Trump-led disclosure initiative on extraterrestrial matters as a "Fake, but kind of Real Alien Disclosure Op," treating it as one of the week's principal narrative deployments.[27] The episode paired that segment with the Epstein Web's expansion and the prospective United States–[[iran|Iran]] convergence, and its subtitle linked Trump to "Deep State Burn Cards & the Return of Honor Culture."[27]

## Arctic, Venezuela and the banks (January 2026)

Trump escalated diplomatic tensions with close European allies by announcing a 10% tariff, effective February 1, 2026, on imports from eight European countries opposing U.S. efforts to assert greater influence over [[greenland|Greenland]], which he described as crucial to national security.[28] The targets were [[denmark|Denmark]], Norway, Sweden, France, Germany, the United Kingdom, the Netherlands and Finland, and [[european-union|EU]] officials threatened coordinated counter-measures.[28] Badlands framing casts the move as a keystone of a new [[pax-americana|Pax Americana]] and as part of a shared "Power is Power" refrain uniting Trump with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] in what Burning Bright calls the [[sovereign-trinity|Sovereign Trinity]].[28]

On Venezuela, Trump signed an executive order on January 9, 2026 titled "Safeguarding Venezuelan Oil Revenue for the Good of the American and Venezuelan People," shielding Venezuelan oil revenues held in U.S. Treasury accounts from seizure or judicial attachment, which the [[white-house|White House]] said would protect funds supporting U.S. foreign policy goals and Venezuela's economic stabilization.[28] Asked why he backed acting president [[delcy-rodriguez|Delcy Rodríguez]] over opposition leader [[maria-corina-machado|María Corina Machado]], he answered by invoking Iraq, where firing the police and generals "ended up being ISIS."[28] Together with Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]]'s lifting of sanctions, Badlands reads these steps as a move against the global banking system.[28]

Domestically, Trump suggested in a January Reuters interview that the country "shouldn't even have an election" in the [[2026-midterm-elections|2026 midterms]] given his administration's success — remarks his press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] called facetious.[28] He also promoted a $50 billion rural health plan and warned House Republicans that losing the midterms could mean impeachment proceedings.[28] His administration, with a bipartisan coalition of governors, pressed [[pjm-interconnection|PJM Interconnection]] to expand power supply and make AI data center operators help finance up to $15 billion in new generation.[28]

## Ceasefire diplomacy, FISA renewal, and the war economy (April 2026)

In April 2026 Trump announced that Israel and Lebanon had agreed to a ten-day ceasefire beginning at 5 p.m. EST on Tuesday, following what he called "excellent conversations" with Lebanese President [[joseph-aoun|Joseph Aoun]] and Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]].[29] The announcement came after Israeli and Lebanese officials met in Washington, D.C. for the first time in thirty-four years alongside Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]], and Trump directed Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], Rubio, and Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff [[dan-caine|Dan Caine]] to work with both nations toward a lasting peace agreement.[29] Trump framed the effort as the tenth war he had solved worldwide, writing "let's, GET IT DONE!"[29]

Commentator [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] described the move as Trump running the ball down the field while others bickered, noting the implication of a peace signing in Islamabad, and contrasted it with [[alan-dershowitz|Alan Dershowitz]]'s public plea for Netanyahu to keep fighting "even if it means confrontation with President Trump" — reading Trump as decoupling himself from the conflict while Netanyahu prepared to fight alone.[29]

Separately, Trump urged [[congress|Congress]] to pass a "clean" extension of [[section-702|Section 702]] of the Foreign Intelligence Surveillance Act, potentially around eighteen months, calling on Republican lawmakers to unify behind the authority ahead of its expiration and arguing it was essential to military and intelligence successes including operations tied to [[iran|Iran]] and Venezuela.[29] The push marked a sharp reversal from his earlier criticism of FISA authorities and collided with bipartisan resistance from lawmakers demanding warrant requirements and tighter oversight.[29] In commentary on the emerging [[war-economy|war economy]], [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that sovereign leaders — Trump foremost among them — were being handed public consent to redirect funding from older parasitic systems into a reimagined twenty-first-century industrial base.[29]

## July 2026 election fraud speech

On the Thursday night preceding 18 July 2026, Trump delivered a speech on election fraud that Badlands Media described as seismic.[30] The twenty-fifth episode of [[badlands-blitz|The Blitz]], titled "It's Treason, Then ...", was given over entirely to the speech, with hosts [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] and [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] spending the full hour and change on its content and consequences.[30] They assessed the address as carrying major implications for the [[deep-state|Deep State]] cabal and as marking the beginnings of a true phase shift in both the [[shadow-war|Shadow War]] and the [[information-war|Information War]] at once.[30]

## March 2026: Iran diplomacy, sanctions waivers, and appointments

In late March 2026 Trump pointed to "productive conversations" with [[iran|Iran]] even as Iranian officials denied direct negotiations, with indirect channels through Pakistan and other intermediaries carrying a US-backed ceasefire framework relayed to Tehran and publicly detailed on March 25.[31] Badlands contributor GhostofBasedPatrickHenry reported that Trump said the Iranians had given him a gift signaling a deal was possible, that his administration had identified Iranian officials it trusted, that he expected Iran to deny the talks, and that his administration was removing Iranian leaders' names from its kill list — read as evidence the engagement was real.[31]

Trump also issued temporary waivers easing sanctions on seaborne [[russia|Russian]] oil amid energy price spikes triggered by disruption at the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]], a move legacy outlets cast as accidentally fueling Putin's war machine while he was distracted by Middle East commitments.[31] Badlands contributor Burning Bright rejected that framing, describing the waivers as part of "the methodical unwind of the rules-based international order" and a controlled release of pressure valves across theaters.[31]

The same week, his administration announced appointments to the [[presidents-council-of-advisors-on-science-and-technology|President's Council of Advisors on Science and Technology]], and a federal appeals court upheld his administration's reading of immigration detention law, the [[us-court-of-appeals-for-the-eighth-circuit|Eighth Circuit]] ruling 2-1 that detention is mandatory in most deportation cases after a similar [[us-court-of-appeals-for-the-fifth-circuit|Fifth Circuit]] decision a month earlier.[31] Separately, the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] sought [[house-intelligence-committee|House Intelligence Committee]] records tied to [[john-brennan|John Brennan]], whom Trump had stripped of security clearance in 2018 and again in 2025.[31]

## July 2026: EAC dismissals, NATO in Ankara, and the alleged Iranian plot

On Thursday, July 9, 2026, Trump dismissed the remaining members of the [[election-assistance-commission|US Election Assistance Commission]], the independent federal agency that certifies voting systems and administers federal election grants; one Republican commissioner resigned and the two Democratic commissioners were notified by email of their termination, with no replacement nominees announced.[32] His administration also continued pursuing efforts to encourage states to adopt additional election integrity measures through federal grant conditions, while a federal judge ordered the [[department-of-homeland-security|Department of Homeland Security]] to keep its citizenship verification database offline pending litigation.[32]

Earlier in the week Trump attended a [[nato|NATO]] summit in Ankara, where he repeated high praise for President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Erdoğan]] and [[turkey|Turkey]] — praise that Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] said drew condemnation from the Israeli diaspora and "Mossad Media assets," and which he linked to the Ukrainian strike on the [[blue-stream-pipeline|Blue Stream]] pipeline's compressor station.[32] Trump also highlighted his relationships with other regional Muslim leaders, including Syria's [[ahmed-al-sharaa|al-Sharaa]].[32]

Israeli intelligence reportedly informed US officials of a new [[iran|Iranian]] plot to assassinate Trump, described as consistent with longstanding Iranian vows to avenge the 2020 strike that killed [[qassem-soleimani|Qassem Soleimani]]; speaking at the summit, Trump acknowledged he remains a target and said he had been aware of such risks for years.[32] GhostofBasedPatrickHenry argued Trump had deliberately seeded the narrative to bait [[mossad|Mossad]] Media and the NeoCon wing of the [[uniparty|Uniparty]], and that [[israel|Israel]] should not be ruled out as a suspect in any attempt on Trump's life.[32]

On markets, [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] credited [[trumps-discombobulator|Trump's Discombobulator]] — six months of alternating war and peace signaling, plus direct threats to the US oil cartel — with forcing futures and oil markets to price actual supply and demand rather than stories, arguing Trump "has NEVER been waging a real war" but a War of Stories.[32] Meanwhile the administration advanced a [[federal-research-grant-overhaul|federal research grant overhaul]] and installed climate critic [[david-legates|David Legates]] atop a new [[white-house|White House]] climate office, and the [[department-of-labor|Department of Labor]] Inspector General opened an [[h-1b-visa-program|H-1B]] fraud and human trafficking investigation aligned with Trump's promise to reinvest in American workers.[32]

## January 2026: cartels, the Fed, and the world order

Trump said the United States may begin conducting land strikes against drug cartels operating in Mexico, arguing that cartels running drug operations across the US–Mexico border pose a direct national security threat and that they were effectively running the Mexican government.[33] The remarks alarmed officials in Mexico, who warned against violations of national sovereignty, and drew criticism from Colombian President [[gustavo-petro|Gustavo Petro]]; Trump subsequently posted that he had spoken with Petro by phone and invited him to the [[white-house|White House]].[33] Badlands recalled that Trump had accused Mexican President [[claudia-sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]] of being afraid of the cartels after she rejected his offer to send US troops, and contrasted her with her predecessor Andrés Manuel López Obrador, who deployed 29,000 troops to the border.[33]

On monetary policy, Trump stated that a decision had been made on a nominee to succeed [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] Chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] when Powell's term ends in May 2026, though no formal nomination had been announced.[33] His attempted removal of Fed Governor [[lisa-cook|Lisa Cook]] was headed to the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]], a case Badlands framed as a test of whether executive power under Article II can be insulated from the president by statute.[33] Burning Bright described Trump's mortgage buy-back program as the first in an expected cascade of moves showing he does not need Powell.[33]

Internationally, Trump withdrew the United States from 66 international organizations, half of them [[united-nations|United Nations]] bodies including the climate change platform, prompting the UN spokesman to threaten revoking the US vote over unpaid dues.[33] European leaders — including [[frank-walter-steinmeier|Frank-Walter Steinmeier]], [[emmanuel-macron|Emmanuel Macron]], and [[kaja-kallas|Kaja Kallas]] — publicly warned that his policies were eroding the postwar order, while [[robert-fico|Robert Fico]] said the [[nicolas-maduro|Maduro]] operation had ended it outright.[33] Badlands read the whole sequence as deliberate acceleration.[33]

## Board of Peace, Greenland and the Somali fraud scandal (January 2026)

Trump launched the [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]], a new international body chaired by him and initially established to oversee postwar reconstruction and governance in Gaza under a fragile ceasefire, backed by a November 2025 [[united-nations|UN]] Security Council resolution and mandated until the end of 2027.[35] He scheduled the signing ceremony during the [[world-economic-forum|World Economic Forum]] meeting in Davos and invited leaders of roughly 60 countries, including [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], prompting accusations that he was building a rival to the United Nations.[35]

His renewed campaign to bring [[greenland|Greenland]] under U.S. control — citing Arctic security against Russian and Chinese influence, rare earth minerals, and 150 years of American interest — escalated into the most serious transatlantic crisis in decades, with threatened additional 10–15% tariffs effective February 1 on eight European nations that had deployed forces to the island.[35] In a letter to Norwegian Prime Minister [[jonas-gahr-store|Jonas Gahr Støre]], Trump linked [[norway|Norway]]'s refusal to award him the Nobel Peace Prize for "stopping 8 wars" to a policy shift, writing that he "no longer feel[s] an obligation to think purely of peace."[35]

On January 19, 2026 Trump posted on Truth Social targeting Representative [[ilhan-omar|Ilhan Omar]] (D-MN), suggesting she be jailed or "sent back to Somalia" over the "$19 Billion Dollars in Minnesota Somalia Fraud" and accusing her of knowing "everything" about it.[35] His administration backed the [[scam-act|SCAM Act]] expanding denaturalization powers in response to the [[minnesota-somali-fraud-hearings|Minnesota fraud scandal]].[35]

## June 2026: executive power and Iran diplomacy
In late June 2026 the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] handed Trump a major victory, ruling 6-3 that presidents have broad authority to remove Senate-confirmed leaders of executive branch agencies and overturning a 91-year-old precedent permitting [[congress|Congress]] to confine the removal power to "inefficiency, neglect of duty, or malfeasance in office."[36] A separate 5-4 ruling declined to immediately back his removal of [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] Governor [[lisa-cook|Lisa Cook]], sending the case back to a lower court without barring her eventual removal.[36] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the emphasis on shoring up presidential authority undercuts claims that winning the midterms is paramount, since Trump's own conduct suggests he regards Congress — including the "Uniparty GOP" — as diametrically opposed to his agenda.[36]

Trump also said that [[iran|Iran]] had requested a meeting with the United States and announced talks in Doha, [[qatar|Qatar]], with Special Envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] and [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]] traveling to advance implementation of the US-Iran memorandum of understanding — an account Tehran publicly contradicted.[36]

## The "discombobulator" and the June 2026 Iran pivot

In June 2026 Trump cancelled a threatened round of strikes on [[iran|Iran]] days after warning the US would "bomb the shit out of them," announcing instead that a major peace deal — reportedly to be called the [[islamabad-agreement|Islamabad agreement]] — could be signed imminently.[34] Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued that Trump's whiplash messaging was not aimed at the public or at "waking up the normies," but at shattering the composure of rivals inside the GOP who had already anointed themselves as his successors.[34]

The Brief catalogued the resulting fractures: [[nancy-mace|Nancy Mace]], who finished last in the South Carolina gubernatorial primary after Trump endorsed a rival and then vowed revenge and backed [[alan-wilson|Alan Wilson]] against Trump-endorsed [[pamela-evette|Pamela Evette]]; [[mark-levin|Mark Levin]], who publicly sided with second-hand Iranian accounts over Trump's; [[lindsey-graham|Lindsey Graham]], who stayed silent until his Trump-endorsed primary win and then implied Trump was a chickenhawk; and [[laura-loomer|Laura Loomer]], who circulated Israeli claims that [[israel|Israel]] was not party to the deal.[34] Badlands presented this as vindication of the thesis that Trump "had to activate the discombobulator" to expose conditional allies.[34]

On the signing itself, the Brief noted that four Air Force C-17s had departed Israel for Europe ahead of a Geneva ceremony reportedly to be attended by Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], and expressed concern that Trump's absence and the European venue signalled the accord might not be final.[34]

## Homeland security funding and trafficking enforcement

Trump signed a three-year immigration and border-security package — identified in Badlands commentary as the [[secure-america-act|Secure America Act]] — that the administration said left the [[department-of-homeland-security|Department of Homeland Security]] fully funded, allocating roughly $70 billion in additional immigration-enforcement funding for DHS, [[immigration-and-customs-enforcement|ICE]], and [[customs-and-border-protection|Customs and Border Protection]] through the remainder of his term.[34] Contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the signing as a president funding "the hunters rather than the networks," arriving alongside DHS disclosures on the scale of [[child-trafficking|child trafficking]], while arguing that the surrounding [[epstein-narrative|Epstein narrative]] functions partly as engineered distraction from that campaign.[34]

## February 2026: elections, Israel, Epstein, climate, and allies

In February 2026, court filings revealed that the [[fbi|FBI]] was investigating [[fulton-county|Fulton County]]'s handling of 2020 ballots — a race Trump lost in Georgia by a narrow margin — after a referral from his appointed election security director [[kurt-olsen|Kurt Olsen]], with Director of National Intelligence [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] attending the January warrant execution at his request.[37]

Ahead of a Wednesday meeting with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], Trump said Netanyahu "wants a good deal" with [[iran|Iran]] on its nuclear program, called the Oman-mediated talks "very different" from past negotiations, and expressed optimism that Iran "wants to make a deal very badly" while warning of tough military action, including additional carrier deployments, if diplomacy fails.[37] He simultaneously broke with Israel on the [[west-bank-annexation|West Bank measures]], telling journalist Barak Ravid: "I am against annexation," and adding, "We have enough things to think about now. We don't need to be dealing with the West Bank."[37]

The [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]]'s release of millions of pages of [[jeffrey-epstein|Epstein]] files placed Trump at the center of the renewed [[epstein-narrative|Epstein narrative]]: a 2019 FBI interview recorded his 2006 call to Palm Beach police chief [[michael-reiter|Michael Reiter]] saying "everyone" knew Epstein was abusing teenage girls, calling [[ghislaine-maxwell|Ghislaine Maxwell]] "evil," and describing banning Epstein from Mar-a-Lago.[37] Maxwell's legal team indicated she would testify if granted clemency by Trump.[37] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump was immune to the narrative's effects and cited an Epstein email calling Trump "the dog that didn't bark."[37]

On climate, Trump prepared to headline a White House ceremony with EPA Administrator [[lee-zeldin|Lee Zeldin]] finalizing the rescission of the 2009 [[endangerment-finding|endangerment finding]], billed as the largest act of deregulation in U.S. history.[37]

With allies, Trump threatened to block the opening of the $4.6 billion [[gordie-howe-international-bridge|Gordie Howe International Bridge]], claiming it had "virtually no US content" and warning of 100% tariffs over Canadian trade plans with China; Prime Minister [[mark-carney|Mark Carney]] described their conversation as positive.[37] The aftermath of his retracted threats to acquire [[greenland|Greenland]], his dismissal of [[nato|NATO]] allies' Afghanistan sacrifices, and related embassy incidents fueled European perceptions of the United States as a rival, with unfavorable views exceeding 60% in several nations and peaking at 84% in Denmark.[37] He also met Colombian President [[gustavo-petro|Gustavo Petro]] at the White House, where Petro proposed a joint international operation against the financiers atop organized crime.[37]

## April 2026: Hormuz leverage, Lebanon ceasefire, and marijuana rescheduling

Trump stated that [[iran|Iran]]'s navy lay at the bottom of the sea, its air force was demolished, its air defenses and radar were gone, and its leaders were no longer present, declaring that the United States had "all the time in the world" while time was not on Iran's side.[38] He said Iran was "having a very hard time figuring out who their leader is," that "the infighting is ... CRAZY," and that the US had "total control" of the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]], which would remain "sealed up tight" until a deal was struck.[38] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that the resulting squeeze falls not on Iran but on the [[invisible-enemy|Invisible Enemy]] and its European and Asian dependencies, as the United States absorbs the energy shock and redirects capital inward.[38]

Trump also announced a three-week extension of the Israel–Hezbollah ceasefire following an Oval Office meeting with Israeli and Lebanese representatives, said the United States would work with [[lebanon|Lebanon]] to protect itself from [[hezbollah|Hezbollah]], and expressed plans to host [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] and President [[joseph-aoun|Joseph Aoun]].[38] He declared that no nuclear weapons would be used — a rebuke to [[mark-levin|Mark Levin]] and others calling for him to nuke Iran — and reiterated that nobody would pressure him into a timeline on his peace negotiations, despite Israeli media reporting a Sunday deadline.[38] Badlands commentary read his repeated line that "Israel will have to defend itself" as egging [[israel|Israel]] on to fight alone while he negotiated with the very entities Israel sought to destroy.[38]

Domestically, his administration reclassified state-licensed medical marijuana from Schedule I to Schedule III, with Acting Attorney General [[todd-blanche|Todd Blanche]] crediting Trump's decisive leadership and a December 2025 executive order that removed federal hurdles to research and directed an expedited rescheduling process.[38] The [[medical-marijuana-rescheduling|reclassification]] followed years of stalled efforts under the prior administration and aligned with Trump's campaign promises on American healthcare.[38]

## The 2026 Iran campaign

In March 2026 Trump ordered U.S. military strikes on [[iran|Iran]] in coordination with [[israel|Israel]], opening a [[iran-war|war]] that reached its fifth day within the week.[39] He estimated the campaign would last four to five weeks, declined to rule out ground forces, and had officials insist it would neither become an endless war nor aim at regime change.[39] Speaking at an Oval Office press conference with German Chancellor [[friedrich-merz|Friedrich Merz]], Trump said he may have "forced Israel's hand" into war with Iran's "lunatics," claiming Tehran was preparing to attack first and that deadlocked nuclear talks left no alternative.[39] He denied that Israel had dragged the United States into the conflict, noting fifteen conversations with Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] over two months before granting a green light, confirmed the death of Supreme Leader [[ali-khamenei|Ayatollah Ali Khamenei]] in an Israeli strike, and said most potential future Iranian leaders were already dead.[39] He further urged Iranian dissidents not to protest during the campaign.[39]

Badlands contributor Burning Bright argued that Trump had deliberately placed the entire information environment into chaos, creating not another conventional Forever War but a "Forever War Narrative" that he was daring the media machine to sustain — leaving the institutional left, the [[neoconservatives|NeoCon establishment]], and the America First contingent all scrambling to respond.[39]

## Persian Gulf shipping guarantees

Trump announced that the United States would immediately provide political risk insurance and financial guarantees at "very reasonable" prices for all maritime trade transiting the Persian Gulf, delivered through the [[development-finance-corporation|Development Finance Corporation]], and that the U.S. Navy would escort tankers through the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]] "if necessary."[39] Oil prices tumbled on the announcement, and Trump predicted prices would spike only temporarily before falling below pre-war levels.[39] Commentators tied the initiative to his long-running criticism of the insurance industry's exploitation of Americans, and to Colombian President [[gustavo-petro|Gustavo Petro]]'s claims of joint U.S.–Colombian operations against cartel financiers.[39]

## Domestic front

Domestically, the administration's mass deportation program brought Homeland Security Secretary [[kristi-noem|Kristi Noem]] before the [[senate-judiciary-committee|Senate Judiciary Committee]] on March 3, 2026, where she faced bipartisan criticism while border enforcement under [[tom-homan|Tom Homan]] drew comparative praise.[39] Senator [[thom-tillis|Thom Tillis]] used the hearing to attack Trump allies including Stephen Miller and Corey Lewandowski.[39] Meanwhile the [[2026-democrat-primary-civil-war|Democratic primary civil war]] ahead of the [[2026-midterm-elections|2026 midterms]] was read by Badlands writers as the [[uniparty|uniparty]] left's attempt to appear stable enough to cast Trump and his supporters as extremists.[39]

## Iran strikes and clemency advocacy (March 2026)

On Friday, February 27, 2026, the United States joined [[israel|Israel]] in military strikes against [[iran|Iran]], and Trump said he ordered US forces to join the attack because he believed Iran was preparing to strike first.[40] His remarks followed Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]]'s statement that the United States acted because it expected Iran would retaliate against Israel's planned action and potentially target US forces.[40] Iran responded with missile and drone strikes on locations in Israel and US-linked sites in Gulf states.[40] Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] sought clarification from the [[white-house|White House]] after learning that administration officials might be communicating with the Iranian government, amid Israeli concern that Washington might pursue a ceasefire before Israel achieved its war objectives.[40]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump's "Iranian Knot" was forcing the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] from the periphery onto center stage, and that Trump was giving the Globalists the appearance of a geopolitical fracture while conducting pre-emptive narrative disarmament — stripping Western neoconservatives of their actualizing premise, in what he called potentially Trump's greatest legacy.[40]

On Monday, March 2, 2026, Trump posted "Free Tina Peters" on Truth Social, catalyzing a national argument over sentencing disparities in Colorado and prompting Governor [[jared-polis|Jared Polis]] to signal openness to commuting [[tina-peters|Tina Peters]]' nine-year sentence.[40] In the same period, Trump's cartel policy was credited by Badlands commentators with producing joint [[united-states-southern-command|SOUTHCOM]] operations with [[ecuador|Ecuador]] against narco-terrorists, following his White House meeting with Colombian President [[gustavo-petro|Gustavo Petro]].[40] The [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]]'s unanimous asylum-deference ruling that week was likewise characterized as a legal win for his administration.[40]

## Correspondents' Dinner and 2028 tease (July 2026)

Trump attended the rescheduled [[white-house-correspondents-association-dinner|White House Correspondents' Association Dinner]] on July 24, 2026 at the Waldorf Astoria in Washington, D.C., after the original event was postponed following an April shooting.[41] He joked that the audience represented "the really largest group of Trump derangement syndrome people ever put together at one time," and his address — more than an hour long, per ABC News — alternated between calls for cooperation with the press and criticism of journalists and political opponents.[41] He closed by donning a red "Trump 2028" hat as part of a joke about another presidential campaign, a possibility Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] noted he is doing nothing to distance himself from.[41] Targets of his jokes included Adam Schiff, Chris Christie, Kaitlan Collins, [[ilhan-omar|Ilhan Omar]], RFK Jr., and [[bari-weiss|Bari Weiss]], whom he called "a gay dictator" over CBS News.[41]

During the same period Trump publicly defended Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] against New York Mayor [[zohran-mamdani|Zohran Mamdani]]'s stated intent to arrest him, saying Netanyahu would not be arrested while visiting the United States, amid Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]]'s campaign to dismantle the [[international-criminal-court|International Criminal Court]].[41] Badlands framed the sequence — culminating in [[venezuela|Venezuela]]'s ICC withdrawal being used by the [[state-department|State Department]] as a rallying cry — as a demonstration of Trump's ability to turn the Deep State against itself.[41] Separately, the [[american-bar-association|American Bar Association]] began rescinding its DEI requirements for law schools after Trump signed an executive order addressing such mandates.[41]

## June 2026: halting the Israel-Iran exchange

In early June 2026 Trump intervened to prevent a broader renewal of fighting between [[israel|Israel]] and [[iran|Iran]], warning Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] that Israel could find itself "on your own very soon" if it expanded military operations — comments given to Axios and later confirmed by Reuters.[42] Netanyahu subsequently canceled what would have been Israel's largest strike on Iran since the April ceasefire.[42] On June 8 Trump publicly called on both countries to "immediately stop shooting," spoke again with Netanyahu to ask him to halt planned strikes, and said Iran had told Washington it did not intend further attacks and asked that the message be relayed to Israel.[42]

[[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] cited the episode as proof that Trump had been "waging a war against Israel, using Iran as his [perhaps unwitting] proxy," and recalled Trump's earlier line to a reporter: "No, I might have forced [Israel's] hand."[42] [[mark-levin|Mark Levin]]'s public criticism of Trump for "bashing the Prime Minister of Israel" was offered as further evidence of the rupture.[42]

## Elections and the Los Angeles primary

In Badlands coverage of the [[2026-los-angeles-mayoral-election|2026 Los Angeles mayoral primary]], [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described Trump as a president who "has spent years calling out the same machine" and who "has called the theft what it is," with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] amplifying his signal.[42] Burning Bright argued that Trump's November 2024 victory did not disprove the position that American elections are structurally fraudulent, and that only by making that position explicit and widespread does Trump receive the popular mandate he has requested to dismantle the machinery.[42]

## Denaturalization campaign

On June 8, 2026 the [[trump-administration|Trump administration]] announced it was seeking to revoke the citizenship of 17 naturalized U.S. citizens accused of immigration fraud or other crimes, describing the effort as the largest [[2026-denaturalization-campaign|denaturalization campaign]] ever undertaken by the federal government.[42]

## Chagos Islands purchase proposal

U.S. officials under Trump discussed a proposal that could lead to the United States purchasing the [[chagos-islands|Chagos Islands]] from [[mauritius|Mauritius]] after the territory is transferred from British control, according to a Telegraph report cited by RT.[42] Trump had previously criticized the UK–Mauritius agreement as "an act of great stupidity" and argued it posed national security concerns.[42] Badlands commentary framed the move as bypassing London to deal directly with Mauritius over [[diego-garcia|Diego Garcia]], part of an apparent pattern of "dismantling the remnants of the old British Empire."[42]

## Easter 2026: the Hormuz ultimatum and the NATO squeeze

On Easter Sunday 2026, Trump said the United States would begin targeting Iranian power plants and bridges from Tuesday evening if [[iran|Iran]] did not reopen the [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]], setting a deadline of 8:00 p.m. Eastern Time after extending a prior ultimatum.[43] The threat, delivered in a profanity-laced post that also praised Allah, came as Washington and Tehran weighed a two-phase ceasefire framework assembled by [[pakistan|Pakistan]] and formalized through a memorandum of understanding.[43] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] wrote that Trump was promoting the ultimatum "like a pay-per-view fight night," pairing cartoonish rhetoric with a simultaneous statement to [[fox-news|Fox News]] that [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] and the Iranians were close to a deal that could land by Monday — evidence for the [[fake-iran-war|fake Iran war]] reading.[43]

Trump also said the United States had attempted to send weapons to anti-government protesters in Iran earlier in 2026, routed through [[kurds|Kurdish]] intermediaries, and that Kurdish groups may have kept them instead of delivering them.[43] Within [[nato|NATO]], he criticized European allies for refusing to support the campaign — including their refusal to deploy naval forces to Hormuz — and questioned continued U.S. commitment to the alliance, though withdrawal would require congressional approval.[43] Secretary-General [[mark-rutte|Mark Rutte]] was expected in Washington to ease tensions.[43]

Separately, Badlands noted that [[kanye-west|Kanye West]]'s debut album — carrying the hit "Jesus Walks" — was released in 2004 two weeks after Trump debuted the first episode of The Apprentice.[43] Commentators also observed that Trump had adopted the "Little Brother/Big Brother" metaphor used by Badlands to describe the U.S. relationship with [[israel|Israel]].[43]

## Abraham Accords expansion and Iran framework (May 2026)

In May 2026 Trump publicly signal-boosted an expanded [[abraham-accords|Abraham Accords]], saying that after discussions with the leaders of Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates, Qatar, Pakistan, Türkiye, Egypt, Jordan and Bahrain it "should be mandatory" that all of those countries simultaneously sign on, while extending the framework to [[iran|Iran]] as part of a settlement.[44] He warned that negotiations with Tehran would end in "a Great Deal for all or, no Deal at all — Back to the Battlefront and shooting, but bigger and stronger than ever before."[44] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the moment as the culmination of an argument he first made in November 2023 — that Trump's "Impossible Task" was peace itself, with the Israel–Iran conflict as the Gordian knot — and described Trump as calling for a "Macro Region of Responsibility" in the Middle East that includes the regime he is ostensibly fighting.[44]

Days later Trump proposed that Iran's enriched uranium, which he calls "nuclear dust," be either turned over to the United States for destruction or "destroyed in place" in coordination with Iran under international observation, tying the question to ceasefire and [[strait-of-hormuz|Strait of Hormuz]] negotiations.[44] [[ghost-of-patrick-henry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read the proposal as Trump bucking Israeli demands for a ground extraction operation, calling the approach "the Venezuela Model" and describing Trump as running an exposure operation against subversive elements inside the US government "by playing the heel."[44]

Separately, an Iraqi national with alleged [[islamic-revolutionary-guard-corps|IRGC]] ties, [[mohammad-baqer-al-saadi|Mohammad Baqer Saad Dawood Al-Saadi]], was accused of plotting to assassinate Trump's daughter [[ivanka-trump|Ivanka Trump]] in retaliation for Trump's 2020 order of the drone strike that killed [[qassem-soleimani|Qasem Soleimani]].[44]

## July 2026: World Cup, Canada, broadcast licenses and Saudi nuclear

Trump was credited by Badlands with bringing the [[2026-fifa-world-cup|2026 FIFA World Cup]] to the United States through a contract secured during his first term, an event the Brief said "brought the world to the US during our 250th Freedom celebration."[45] He attended the final at MetLife Stadium, where a photograph of his box prompted readers to identify Saudi Defense Minister [[khalid-bin-salman|Khalid bin Salman]].[45]

Amid the [[canadian-wildfire-smoke-crisis-2026|Canadian wildfire smoke crisis]], Trump declared: "We are holding Canada responsible…the United States is being unnecessarily invaded by filthy, polluted, and unhealthy air, the quality of which is dangerous, and totally unacceptable!"[45] Badlands commentary read the statement as the president "taking on the climate cult," framing forest-management neglect rather than the [[climate-change-narrative|climate change narrative]] as the cause of the fires.[45]

Trump also said networks that declined to air his primetime address on election security should have their broadcast licenses revoked, naming ABC and NBC and alleging a "plot"; [[federal-communications-commission|FCC]] Commissioner [[anna-gomez|Anna Gomez]] called the proposal unconstitutional.[45] Separately, his administration tentatively agreed to the [[us-saudi-civilian-nuclear-cooperation-agreement|US–Saudi civilian nuclear cooperation agreement]] permitting Saudi uranium enrichment, which he had not signed as of July 2026.[45] In regional diplomacy, he met Syrian President [[ahmed-al-sharaa|Ahmed Al-Sharaa]] at the NATO summit in Ankara and announced intent to remove Syria from the state sponsor of terrorism list, then hosted Iraqi Prime Minister [[ali-al-zaidi|Ali Al-Zaidi]] and backed an Iraq–Syria oil pipeline bypassing the Strait of Hormuz and the Red Sea.[45]

## May 2026 developments

An August 10, 2022 [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] email from [[patty-stemler|Patty Stemler]] to [[sophia-brill|Sophia Brill]], surfaced in May 2026, showed internal doubt over whether Trump had declassified the documents seized in the [[mar-a-lago|Mar-a-Lago]] raid, with Stemler observing that "the President as Commander in Chief" is not necessarily bound by standard declassification procedures.[46] Trump's legal team, including [[evan-corcoran|Evan Corcoran]], had asserted in May 2022 that he held broad declassification authority and had issued a standing order treating documents removed to his residence as declassified.[46]

The Trump administration's pressure campaign on [[cuba|Cuba]] — a tightened blockade, the state-sponsor-of-terrorism designation and the indictment of [[raul-castro|Raúl Castro]] — formed the backdrop to the [[office-of-foreign-assets-control|OFAC]] probe of the [[nuestra-america-convoy|Nuestra America Convoy]] and subpoenas of [[hasan-piker|Hasan Piker]] and [[medea-benjamin|Medea Benjamin]].[46]

In the [[2026-iran-deal-negotiations|Iran negotiations]], Israel's national security minister said [[israel|Israel]] would not let Trump sign a "bad deal," and Badlands predicted the GOP would turn on Trump once any agreement was signed.[46] Trump was also noted for consistently praising Pakistan's leadership, in contrast to [[lindsey-graham|Lindsey Graham]]'s attack on Defence Minister [[khawaja-asif|Khawaja Asif]].[46]

Domestically, Trump's endorsement of [[ken-paxton|Ken Paxton]] days before early voting was described as decisive in Paxton's [[2026-texas-senate-primary-runoff|63%-37% runoff victory]] over Senator [[john-cornyn|John Cornyn]] for the Republican U.S. Senate nomination in Texas.[46]

## Election nationalization push and February 2026 diplomacy

In early February 2026 Trump publicly called on Republicans to "take over" and "nationalize" election administration in at least 15 states, arguing this was needed to prevent undocumented immigrants from voting and to address what he described as inaccurate vote counting.[47] He endorsed two bills: the [[save-america-act|Save America Act]], mandating proof of citizenship for voter registration and imposing restrictions on mail-in voting, and the [[mega-act|Make Elections Great Again Act]], which would prohibit ranked-choice voting and universal mail ballots and establish national election audits.[47] His administration launched multiple probes into the 2020 election, with DNI [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] leading an intelligence community review parallel to a DOJ criminal investigation; Trump congratulated FBI agents by phone during their raid on a Fulton County, Georgia election facility.[47]

On trade, Trump announced a U.S.-India agreement lowering tariffs on Indian imports from 25% to 18%, contingent on [[india|India]] ceasing purchases of Russian crude and increasing U.S. energy and goods purchases — though New Delhi remained silent and Kremlin spokesman [[dmitry-peskov|Dmitry Peskov]] said Moscow had received no indication of any oil cutoff.[47] U.S. trade partners accelerated alternative arrangements including an India-EU free trade deal and an EU-Mercosur bloc covering over 700 million people, while Trump threatened 100% tariffs on Canada over electric-vehicle policy alignment with China and raised tariffs on South Korea.[47] With the [[new-start-treaty|New START treaty]] set to expire, Trump declined to respond to Russia's September offer of a one-year extension, saying he preferred a new deal including China: "If it expires, it expires. We'll just do a better agreement."[47]
