In April 2023 federal investigators were reported to be asking witnesses whether Trump had shown aides and visitors a map containing sensitive intelligence information that he took with him when he left office.[128] The map was one focus of the broad [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] investigation into his handling of classified documents after departing the [[white-house|White House]], overseen by special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]], who was weighing charges that could include obstruction of justice.[128]

The nature of the map and the information it contained was not clear.[128] One person briefed on the matter said investigators asked about Trump showing the map aboard a plane; another said the questioning suggested he had shown it to at least one adviser after leaving office; a third said it might also have been shown to a journalist writing a book.[128] Earlier reporting indicated investigators had asked about classified material, including maps, being shown to political donors.[128] Badlands contributor [[just-human|Just Human]] argued the story's release was conspicuously timed against the [[discord-classified-documents-leak|Discord leak]] of more than 100 genuinely classified documents.[128]

## January 2025: Transition, Putin Talks, and Inauguration Diplomacy

On January 9, 2025, President-elect Donald Trump said that Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] wanted to meet with him and that preparations were underway, telling reporters at his Mar-a-Lago residence, "He wants to meet, and we are setting it up... President Putin wants to meet. He has said that even publicly and we have to get that war over with. That's a bloody mess."[129] The Kremlin said on January 10 that it welcomed Trump's readiness for talks while noting that plans for a face-to-face meeting would not be drawn up until after his inauguration.[129]

Trump broke with custom by inviting Chinese president [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] to his [[second-inauguration-of-donald-trump|second inauguration]] on January 20, setting off a scramble among foreign leaders for invitations; Xi declined and was expected to send a top emissary instead.[129] El Salvador's [[nayib-bukele|Nayib Bukele]] and former Brazilian president [[jair-bolsonaro|Jair Bolsonaro]] were among those invited.[129]

In early January 2025 Trump posted to Truth Social a video of economist [[jeffrey-sachs|Jeffrey Sachs]] calling Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] a "deep, dark son of a bitch" and accusing him of manipulating US foreign policy and orchestrating endless wars in the Middle East; Trump's aim in promoting the video was not immediately clear.[129] Separately, [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] said he was still weighing [[preemptive-pardons|preemptive pardons]] for individuals Trump had targeted, citing Trump's calls for prosecuting his perceived enemies.[129]

## Easter 2024 culture-war clash and the Diller wake

In late March 2024 the Trump campaign joined House leadership in demanding an apology from the [[white-house|White House]] after President Biden issued a proclamation recognizing March 31 — Easter Sunday that year — as [[transgender-day-of-visibility|Transgender Day of Visibility]].[130] Days later, Trump attended the wake of murdered [[nypd|New York City police]] officer [[jonathan-diller|Jonathan Diller]] in Queens on the same day Biden appeared at a $500,000-per-ticket fundraiser with [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]], [[bill-clinton|Bill Clinton]] and the singer [[lizzo|Lizzo]]; the juxtaposition was widely noted, though critics argued Trump had effectively turned the wake into a campaign stop.[130] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that the contrasts between the two men were becoming "more and more cartoonish as the Info War accelerates toward whatever inflection point November represents."[130]

## 2024 nomination, running-mate speculation, and legal filings

Trump became the presumptive Republican presidential nominee on March 12, 2024, following a string of primary victories, setting up a likely rematch with Biden.[130] Speculation over his running mate intensified after his falling-out with former vice president [[mike-pence|Mike Pence]], who declined to endorse him; at a Fox News town hall in January Trump said, "I can't tell you that really, I mean, I know who it's going to be," while former Secretary of State [[mike-pompeo|Mike Pompeo]] said he would be "pretty inclined" to join the ticket if asked.[130] On March 24, 2024, Trump's legal team filed a seven-page document with the U.S. District Court for the Southern District of Florida in support of his bid to dismiss the [[trump-classified-documents-case|classified documents]] charges on grounds of [[presidential-immunity|presidential immunity]], and twice referred to his time in the White House as his "first term" as president.[130] He then faced a total of 40 felony charges over allegations he illegally retained classified papers after leaving office in January 2021 and obstructed efforts to retrieve them, pleading not guilty to all counts while facing criminal charges in four separate cases.[130]

## Georgia appeal and the RFK debate challenge (May 2024)

On 8 May 2024 the [[georgia-court-of-appeals|Georgia Court of Appeals]] agreed to hear Trump's bid to disqualify [[fulton-county|Fulton County]] District Attorney [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]] from the [[state-of-georgia-v-trump|Georgia election interference case]], in which he was charged with illegally attempting to overturn his loss to [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] in the state's 2020 election.[131] The appeal followed Judge [[scott-mcafee|Scott McAfee]]'s March refusal to remove Willis over her romantic relationship with a member of her prosecution team, and no trial date had been set.[131] Trump attorney Steve Sadow said the former president looked forward to arguing "why the case should be dismissed and Fulton County DA Willis should be disqualified for her misconduct in this unjustified, unwarranted political persecution."[131] Badlands commentary read the decision as part of a pattern of "collapsing Trump trials," invoking the attrition argument associated with Jonathan Turley that a system whose lawfare cases rest on sand will look for off-ramps — and noting that Trump's polling stood at record highs even by establishment measures while TIME ran an "If He Wins" feature on Agenda 47.[131]

That same week independent candidate [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] challenged Trump to a debate at the [[libertarian-party|Libertarian Party]] national convention, praising him for exposing "rigged polling" while claiming his own Zogby survey showed Kennedy narrowly beating Trump head-to-head, and criticising Trump for blowing up the deficit, shutting businesses during COVID, and filling his administration with "swamp creatures."[131]

## Trudeau meeting and the "51st state" remark (November–December 2024)

After Trump threatened sweeping tariffs on Canadian goods, Canadian Prime Minister [[justin-trudeau|Justin Trudeau]] flew unannounced to Mar-a-Lago on Friday, November 29, 2024.[132] Trump was threatening 25% tariffs on both Canada and Mexico over their failure to curb flows of illegal immigrants and illicit drugs into the United States.[132] Trump called the meeting "very productive."[132]

According to two people at the table, Trump was cordial but direct, telling Trudeau that Canada had failed the U.S. border by allowing large quantities of drugs and people across, including illegal immigrants from more than 70 countries.[132] He grew more animated over the U.S. trade deficit with Canada, which he estimated at more than $100 billion, and said he would levy a 25% tariff on all Canadian goods on his first day back in office if the border and deficit issues were not fixed.[132] When Trudeau replied that such a tariff would kill the Canadian economy completely, Trump asked whether Canada could not survive unless it was "ripping off" the United States by $100 billion, then suggested that Canada become the 51st state — prompting nervous laughter from the prime minister and others.[132]

Mexican President [[claudia-sheinbaum|Claudia Sheinbaum]] separately agreed, according to a Trump post on the Wednesday before, to stop unauthorized migration across the border, and Mexican authorities broke up two migrant caravans headed north.[132] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] wrote that the episode showed Trump holding firm while Trudeau capitulated, and predicted that Canadians and Mexicans would ultimately blame their own governments rather than the United States.[132]

## Middle East posture

The [[badlands-news-brief|Badlands News Brief]] noted that Trump had said on multiple occasions that he did not believe any living hostages remained in Gaza, and read his December 2024 post on the death of [[omer-neutra|Omer Maxim Neutra]] as evidence that he understood the hostage narrative to be exhausted.[132] Retired admiral [[james-stavridis|James Stavridis]] attributed the Israel–[[hezbollah|Hezbollah]] ceasefire in part to Trump's impending return, saying leaders in many capitals were "holding their breath."[132] NATO Secretary-General [[mark-rutte|Mark Rutte]] warned Trump in a Financial Times interview that pressuring Ukraine into a "bad" peace deal favorable to Moscow would create a "dire threat" from Iran, China and North Korea.[132]

## Knesset address and Gaza ceasefire (October 2025)

On October 13, 2025 Trump addressed the [[knesset|Knesset]] as the final 20 living hostages were released from [[gaza|Gaza]] under a US-brokered ceasefire, declaring "the historic dawn of the new Middle East" and telling Israeli lawmakers "You've won… now it is time to translate these victories against terrorists on the battlefield into the ultimate prize of peace and prosperity for the entire Middle East."[133] He said [[israel|Israel]] had achieved all it could "by force of arms" and indicated he would carry the same message to regional leaders in Egypt later that day; see [[2025-knesset-address-by-donald-trump|the address]].[133] During the speech he called for Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] to be pardoned in his corruption trial, later telling reporters aboard Air Force One that the intervention was unplanned and prompted by the applause: "He was getting a very good hand, and when they stopped I said, 'why don't you give this guy a pardon?'"[133]

Trump publicly ridiculed former Secretary of State [[antony-blinken|Antony Blinken]]'s claim that the peace framework had been developed by the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration, saying "everybody knows that's a joke… they did such a bad job. That was weak leadership."[133]

## Trade escalation and Venezuela (October 2025)

In the same period Trump said he saw "no reason" to meet President [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] of [[china|China]] in October, threatened restrictions on certain US software exports, and warned of 100% tariffs on Chinese goods from November 1 in response to Beijing's tightening of rare-earth export controls, contributing to market turmoil that included [[2025-silver-short-squeeze|a silver short squeeze]] and record crypto liquidations.[133] Politico separately reported that the administration was weighing an attempt to assassinate Venezuelan President [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] after designating him a drug lord and terrorist, with Trump reportedly intending to use the war on drug cartels as a pretext for removing him from power.[133]

[[steve-bannon|Steve Bannon]], Trump's former White House chief strategist, told The Wall Street Journal that the president's four years "in the wilderness" between 2021 and 2025 had hardened his resolve, saying "he's doing things now he wouldn't have ever considered the first time — he's jackhammering away on levels you haven't seen before," and likening the Republican-controlled [[congress|Congress]] to Russia's ceremonial Duma.[133]

## The March 2023 Manhattan indictment watch

In March 2023 an arrest of Trump by the [[manhattan-district-attorneys-office|Manhattan District Attorney's office]] under [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]] was widely predicted for Tuesday, March 21, but the day passed without action.[134] A purported 2018 letter from [[michael-cohen|Michael Cohen]]'s attorney to the [[federal-election-commission|Federal Election Commission]] — stating that Cohen paid [[stormy-daniels|Stormy Daniels]] with his own personal funds and that the Trump campaign neither participated in nor reimbursed the transaction — was circulated by [[benny-johnson|Benny Johnson]] and reported as "totally exculpatory" material capable of hobbling the case.[134]

[[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] argued that prosecutors knew there was no case and sought only "the show and the mug shot," calling the effort a [[nancy-pelosi|Pelosi]]-style "wrap-up smear" aimed at entrapping reactionary Trump supporters, and urged peaceful mass protest directed at [[george-soros|George Soros]].[134] Separately, [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] grouped Trump with [[vladimir-putin|Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi]] as leaders calling for "peace, reconstruction ... and restitution" amid the shift toward a [[multipolar-world|multipolar world order]].[134]

## Posture on the Israel–Hamas conflict (October 2023)

Writing in the October 30, 2023 [[badlands-news-brief|Badlands News Brief]], [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] assessed that Trump might be publicly backing one side over the other in the [[israel|Israel]]–[[hamas|Hamas]] conflict, which was easy from an optics standpoint when one side were "literal terrorists" and the other represented a sovereign nation.[135] He argued that Trump was nonetheless "towing a line deftly," defending Israel's right to defend itself while refusing to escalate rhetoric toward [[iran|Iran]], a posture mirrored by [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] in their warnings that the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration was threatening global stability.[135] The same commentary noted growing bipartisan agreement that the Biden administration was mishandling the conflict catastrophically, whatever one made of Trump's statements on the theater.[135] Separately, [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] framed [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]]'s election as Speaker as proof of the ascendance of the Trump-inspired MAGA movement within the Republican Party.[135]

## September 2024 campaign: uncommitted outreach and debate refusal

In mid-September 2024 Trump met in Flint, Michigan, with [[hamtramck|Hamtramck]] Mayor [[amer-ghalib|Amer Ghalib]], a Democrat who helped lead the [[uncommitted-movement|uncommitted movement]] in the Democratic primary; Ghalib called the roughly 20-minute meeting "very productive," said it was well received in his heavily Arab-American community, and was weighing an endorsement.[136] Ghalib said the meeting arose because many in Hamtramck, Dearborn, and neighboring Wayne County cities supported Trump and "the shifting dynamics" he was leading, and he was quoted as saying "Trump doesn't want wars."[136] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued Trump's antiwar record was indisputable and that uncommitted Free Palestine voters casting ballots in their own self-interest would plausibly break for him.[136]

On September 21 Trump rejected [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]]'s acceptance of a [[cnn]] debate invitation for October 23, telling a rally in Wilmington, North Carolina, that "the problem with another debate is that it's just too late, voting has already started."[136]

Trump also insisted there should be no stop-gap government funding measure without a mandate that states require proof of citizenship to register to vote; House Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] abandoned that demand to secure a short-term spending deal, which nonetheless included $231 million in added funding for the [[us-secret-service|Secret Service]] after the two assassination attempts against Trump.[136]

## December 2025: pardon, marijuana, and strategy shifts

On 11 December 2025 Trump announced a full pardon of former Colorado clerk [[tina-peters|Tina Peters]] "for her attempts to expose Voter Fraud in the Rigged 2020 Presidential Election," accusing Democrats of ignoring violent crime while prosecuting citizens who wanted "Safe and Secure Elections."[138] Colorado's Democratic officials rejected the pardon's reach over a state conviction, setting up a constitutional confrontation that Badlands commentary said could strengthen Tenth Amendment protections while testing federal pardon power.[138]

The same week Trump was reported to be preparing an executive order directing federal agencies to pursue reclassification of marijuana, loosening federal restrictions to the level of some common prescription painkillers.[138] He discussed the plan with House Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] in a Wednesday phone call from the Oval Office; the move would not legalize or decriminalize the drug but would ease barriers to research and benefit legal cannabis businesses.[138] [[jordan-sather|Jordan Sather]] characterized it as a shift from Schedule I to Schedule III that would "blow so many left wing AND right wing minds."[138]

Trump's new National Security Strategy, released the same month, dropped the sweeping language about China as America's most consequential geopolitical challenge in favour of an emphasis on economic rivalry, and a reported unpublished draft proposed a [[core-5|"Core 5"]] great-power forum with Russia, China, India and Japan to sideline the [[g7|G7]].[138] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the doctrine as a revival of the Monroe Doctrine — see [[monroe-doctrine|Monroe Doctrine]] — and attributed the alarm it provoked among collectivists to their understanding of its implications.[138]

In foreign affairs the administration seized a Venezuelan oil tanker, prompting Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] to publicly back [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]], while a Trump-brokered peace agreement in the [[democratic-republic-of-the-congo|Democratic Republic of the Congo]] failed to prevent [[m23|M23]] rebels from seizing [[uvira|Uvira]].[138] Domestically, Trump's proposal for a mechanism allowing Americans to report on journalists drew criticism as a "snitch line" threatening press freedom.[138]

## Return to X and interview with Elon Musk (August 2024)

On Monday, August 12, 2024, Trump sat for a long-form live interview with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] on [[x-corp|X]], an event [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] covered before, during and after the broadcast and which contributors described as a demonstration that Trump had mastered long-form podcasting.[137] The same day he resumed posting on the platform for the first time since August 24, 2023 — when he circulated his mug shot with the caption "Election interference. Never surrender!" — and before that since January 8, 2021.[137] His return featured three campaign videos emphasizing his vision for the country and attacking his opponent, whom he labeled "San Francisco Radical [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]]," and came amid Harris' rise in polling.[137] Badlands contributors read the convergence as coordinated rather than coincidental within the [[information-war|Info War]].[137] See [[elon-musk-trump-x-interview|Elon Musk–Trump X interview]].[137]

## $100 million claim against the Justice Department (August 2024)

Trump moved in August 2024 to sue the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] for $100 million in damages over the FBI's August 8, 2022 search of his Mar-a-Lago property in Palm Beach, Florida, with his lawyers alleging the raid was carried out with "clear intent to engage in political persecution" and constituted "tortious conduct by the United States against President Trump."[137] The search stemmed from a federal investigation into his alleged improper retention of classified records, after which Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] brought 37 felony counts against him, including willful retention of national defense information, conspiracy to obstruct justice, and false statements; Trump pleaded not guilty to all counts.[137] Judge [[aileen-cannon|Aileen Cannon]] later ruled that Smith had been unlawfully appointed and that Trump had immunity regardless, and the Supreme Court separately ruled on presidential immunity.[137] See [[mar-a-lago-raid|Mar-a-Lago raid]].[137]

## Tips taxation proposal and assassination plot allegations

Trump first proposed eliminating federal taxes on tips in June 2024, an idea Harris embraced that August as both campaigns courted Nevada's tipped workforce.[137] Separately, Pakistani national [[asif-raza-merchant|Asif Raza Merchant]] was charged in a plot to assassinate high-ranking U.S. officials allegedly including Trump, described by Attorney General [[merrick-garland|Merrick Garland]] as part of a broader Iranian effort to avenge the 2020 killing of General [[qassem-soleimani|Qasem Soleimani]] during Trump's administration.[137]

## June 2025: Los Angeles, Musk, and aviation orders

In June 2025 Trump's administration federalized roughly 2,000 [[california-army-national-guard|California Army National Guard]] soldiers and placed some 500 [[united-states-marine-corps|Marines]] in a prepared-to-deploy status amid protests over immigration enforcement raids in Los Angeles, drawing an accusation of escalation from Governor [[gavin-newsom|Gavin Newsom]].[139] The same weekend Trump told NBC News that [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] would face "very serious consequences" if he funded Democratic candidates against Republicans backing the GOP budget bill, said he had no intention of speaking with Musk again, and called him "disrespectful to the office of the President."[139] On 6 June 2025 Trump signed executive orders deregulating electric vertical takeoff and landing aircraft and supersonic flight, alongside orders on American drone capabilities.[139] Trump's 2020 campaign and his lawyers were also among the defendants in the defamation suits brought by [[eric-coomer|Eric Coomer]] over amplification of [[joe-oltmann|Joe Oltmann]]'s election-rigging allegations.[139]

## October 2023: willingness to serve as Speaker

Amid the vacancy created by the [[ouster-of-kevin-mccarthy|removal of Kevin McCarthy]], several conservatives including Rep. [[marjorie-taylor-greene|Marjorie Taylor Greene]] called on Trump to serve as Speaker of the House as one route toward returning him to the presidency.[140] Speaking from a New York courthouse on October 4, 2023, Trump confirmed that he had been approached about becoming the next Speaker of the House of Representatives and said he would be willing to take on the role, emphasizing his commitment to doing "whatever's best for the country and for the Republican Party."[140] [[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] considered the prospect improbable because [[rino|RINOs]] retained the votes to block it, while noting that such a ballot would at least reveal the genuine patriots in [[congress|Congress]]; [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the episode as evidence that Trump's unpredictability is a strategic asset, writing that "Trump's not locked in with them ... they're locked in with him."[140]

Around the same time Trump skipped the first two Republican primary debates and announced he would miss a third, prompting Gov. [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]] to accuse him of being "missing in action" and of adopting "Joe Biden's basement strategy" — an inversion of Trump's own 2020 attack line on [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[140] Burning Bright answered that campaigning against "Uniparty shills & RINO cast-offs" for a presidency Trump never lost was beneath his notice.[140] Elsewhere in the same period, commentary in the [[badlands-news-brief|Badlands News Brief]] blamed the stagflationary spiral in the dollar partly on the Operation Warp Speed-era money printing Trump ordered in 2020.[140]

## New York civil fraud penalty overturned (August 2025)

On Thursday, August 21, 2025, a New York appellate court struck down the half-billion-dollar civil fraud penalty against Trump, his two sons, other executives and their company, ruling the fine "excessive" and in violation of the Eighth Amendment's ban on excessive fines.[141] The penalty stemmed from a February 2024 ruling by Judge [[arthur-engoron|Arthur Engoron]], who had ordered Trump and his co-defendants to pay nearly $364 million in "ill-gotten gains" plus years of interest, bringing the total to roughly $527 million, of which Trump was responsible for nearly 98%.[141] Badlands host [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]] commented that "it's like the whole thing was fake from the beginning," while [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] asked when Engoron would be held accountable for allowing the excessive fine in the first place.[141]

## Visa review and Ukraine diplomacy (August 2025)

In August 2025 the Trump administration announced it was reviewing more than 55 million people holding valid U.S. visas for any violations that could lead to deportation, part of a widening crackdown on foreigners permitted to be in the United States.[141] The [[state-department|State Department]] said all U.S. visa holders are subject to "continuous vetting."[141] In the same period, Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] delivered formal demands to Washington for ending the war in [[ukraine|Ukraine]] following his meeting with Trump in Alaska, and Director of National Intelligence [[tulsi-gabbard|Tulsi Gabbard]] was reported to have cut the [[five-eyes|Five Eyes]] partners off from intelligence on the negotiations.[141] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump and Putin were not merely negotiating but "taking the collective mind along a narrative arc whose endgame is peace," at the expense of the globalist hegemon.[141]

## Pressure on Colorado over Tina Peters (August 2025)

On Thursday, August 21, 2025, Trump re-upped his call for Colorado to free [[tina-peters|Tina Peters]], warning of "harsh measures" if she were not released.[141] On Truth Social he wrote, "FREE TINA PETERS, a brave and innocent Patriot who has been tortured by Crooked Colorado politicians, including the big Mail-In Ballot supporting the governor of the State," and demanded, "Let Tina Peters out of jail, RIGHT NOW."[141] He added that she "did nothing wrong, except catching the Democrats cheat in the Election," described her as an old and very sick woman, and said that if she were not released he would "take harsh measures."[141] He did not specify the measures, though he has previously threatened to withhold funding from states and jurisdictions that do not align with his policies.[141] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] wrote that federal funding is the primary lever available to him, noted the post's timing as the Colorado Legislature opened a special session over a $1.2 billion budget deficit caused by federal cuts, and called it "the art of the deal in action."[141]

## Pressure on the Federal Reserve (August 2025)

As Chairman [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] prepared his final Jackson Hole speech in August 2025, [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described Trump as executing a "pincer move" on the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]], cornering it on both Actual and Narrative fronts so that every decision would accelerate the system's exposure and eventual reset.[141] Trump's April 2 executive order imposing wide-ranging tariffs was taking shape as the central bank came under mounting White House pressure to lower rates, while the administration fast-tracked [[stephen-miran|Stephen Miran]] to a Fed board vacancy and Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] publicly anticipated a "global economic reordering."[141]

## Venezuela, cartels and Gaza (August 2025)

Bolivian President [[luis-arce|Luis Arce]] accused the administration of using the war on drugs as cover for geopolitical control of Latin America after U.S. air and naval forces deployed to the southern Caribbean.[141] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read the friction between Trump's camp and [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] — including a $50 million State Department bounty on Maduro's head — as "kayfabe" providing Maduro narrative shielding while both men targeted the same corrupt commodity-trading executives, such as those at [[glencore|Glencore]].[141] Separately, Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] cited Trump's statement that [[hamas|Hamas]] "can't stay" in Gaza while vowing to conquer the enclave regardless of any hostage deal.[141]

## September 2024 campaign: tariffs, RICO, and early voting

In September 2024 Trump threatened Illinois-based [[john-deere|John Deere]] with a 200 percent tariff on everything it sought to sell into the United States if it proceeded with a plan to shift manufacturing to Mexico, telling an audience of farmers in Pennsylvania that he was "just notifying John Deere right now."[142] The threat was widely read as a signal that he would use tariffs aggressively in a second term.[142]

The same period saw Trump move to revive a civil [[racketeer-influenced-and-corrupt-organizations-act|RICO]] suit against [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] before a federal appeals court, seeking more than $70 million from Clinton and over two dozen other defendants for allegedly attempting to rig the 2016 election, and claiming over $24 million in costs defending against Russian collusion accusations; a federal judge gave him until September 27 to file a brief of up to 10,000 words.[142]

Campaigning in Indiana, Pennsylvania, Trump urged supporters to vote early in the state's imminent early-voting window while simultaneously calling the practice "stupid," reflecting his position that early voting is vulnerable to fraud and helped cost him the 2020 election — a contradiction noted at a time when polls showed a close race with Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]].[142]

## CNN town hall (May 2023)

On May 10, 2023, Trump appeared for 70 minutes on [[cnn|CNN]] in a town hall at [[saint-anselm-college|Saint Anselm College]] before New Hampshire GOP primary voters, moderated by [[kaitlan-collins|Kaitlan Collins]].[143] [[breitbart|Breitbart]] columnist [[john-nolte|John Nolte]] wrote that the performance "might've delivered the final killshot" to a network already struggling in the ratings, saying Trump "put the t-r-u-m-p in 'triumph,'" arrived with a plan, executed it perfectly, and exposed Collins as a "mental lightweight."[143]

Pressed on Russia's war against Ukraine, Trump declined to say who he thought should prevail, telling the audience "I want everybody to stop dying. They're dying. Russians and Ukrainians. I want them to stop dying," and adding "I'll have that done in 24 hours."[143] He said he does not "think in terms of winning and losing" but "in terms of getting it settled so we stop killing all these people."[143]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] of [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] characterized the town hall as a pivotal potential turn in the public narrative around Trump, arguing that the decisive fact was that he was fun, funny and likable — a quality the commentary said American politics lacks.[143] Burning Bright also read Trump's refusal to take sides as consistent with theories of international coordination between Trump, [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and other would-be sovereign leaders, and argued that the exposure of CNN as escalatory warmongers was a win in the "Mind War."[143] Separately, the same commentary noted that the [[joe-biden|Biden]] administration's post-[[title-42|Title 42]] border restrictions were "oddly similar" to Trump's rhetoric from 2015 onward.[143]

## Davenport, Iowa education speech (March 2023)

Speaking in Davenport, Iowa, on Monday, March 13, 2023, as his 2024 presidential campaign kicked into high gear, Trump promised that he would "totally obliterate the DEEP STATE" and declared that "2024 is the final battle."[144] In the same speech he laid out an education platform, saying his administration had studied breaking up the federal [[department-of-education|Department of Education]] and redistributing its functions to the states: "We studied it very closely and I was getting ready to do it, pull the trigger," he said, adding that the long-term goal had become a short-term one.[144]

Badlands contributor [[ryan-delarme|Ryan DeLarme]] used the speech to push back on anonymous online claims that Trump had "allowed many members of the Deep State into his WH" and was "STILL conspiring with them" — evidenced, critics said, by his backing of [[ronna-mcdaniel|Ronna McDaniel]] and [[kevin-mccarthy|Kevin McCarthy]].[144] DeLarme replied that draining the swamp immediately would have left posts to be filled by unprepared patriots, and noted that Trump's speech was infused with confidence and near-excitement even as the nation's infrastructure and economy crumbled.[144] [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] called the proposed dismantling of the education system possibly "the most important blow to the modern-day slave plantation."[144]

## June 2025: Gaza ultimatum, Los Angeles deployment and the Musk feud

In a tense 40-minute phone call with Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] on Monday, June 9, 2025, Trump reportedly told the premier that he must permanently end the war in the Gaza Strip and that attacking Iran was off limits for the time being.[145] He said the [[witkoff-framework|Witkoff framework]] — a roughly 60-day pause in exchange for about half the hostages held by [[hamas|Hamas]] — would not suffice, arguing that ending the war would help both the nuclear negotiations with Iran and normalization talks with Saudi Arabia.[145]

Domestically, Trump federalized California National Guard forces and deployed U.S. Marines to Los Angeles during the [[2025-los-angeles-immigration-enforcement-protests|anti-ICE protests]], drawing a lawsuit from Governor [[gavin-newsom|Gavin Newsom]] against Trump, Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]] and the [[department-of-defense|Department of Defense]].[145] Administration lawyers called Newsom's restraining-order motion "legally meritless," and Judge [[charles-breyer|Charles Breyer]] granted them additional time to respond while Trump addressed Army troops at Fort Bragg, North Carolina.[145] The [[white-house|White House]] framed the operation as removing violent criminal illegal immigrants from Los Angeles streets, while Newsom called it authoritarianism and "a military dragnet."[145]

Trump's public feud with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] over the "Big Beautiful" tax and spending bill also escalated, with Trump saying Musk had "gone crazy" and attributing the rupture to the end of the "EV mandate"; Musk briefly posted a message linking Trump to [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] before deleting it.[145] Separately, Hegseth told the [[house-appropriations-committee|House Appropriations Committee]] that Ukraine military aid would be cut as Trump pursued a negotiated settlement.[145]

## December 2025 controversies and decisions

Trump drew widespread criticism, including from conservatives and supporters, for a social media post blaming filmmaker [[rob-reiner|Rob Reiner]]'s outspoken opposition to him for Reiner's killing, writing that Reiner and his wife [[michele-singer-reiner|Michele Singer Reiner]] were killed "reportedly due to the anger he caused others through his massive, unyielding, and incurable affliction with a mind crippling disease known as TRUMP DERANGEMENT SYNDROME."[146] The unsubstantiated claim came while police were still investigating the deaths as an apparent homicide, and marked a drastic departure from the consolatory role presidents typically play after the death of a public figure.[146] Badlands contributor [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] responded that being politically incorrect is one of Trump's love languages, while [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]] argued the post usefully wrecked the "fake kumbaya" script surrounding [[candace-owens|Candace Owens]] and [[erika-kirk|Erika Kirk]] ahead of the [[turning-point-usa|TPUSA]] convention.[146]

On the same day Trump said his administration was examining whether Israel violated the Gaza ceasefire through the [[killing-of-saad-raad|killing of Hamas official Sa'ad Ra'ad]], with the White House sending a scolding message to Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] warning that Israel would not be permitted to ruin Trump's reputation after he brokered the Gaza deal.[146] He also said he was "considering" reclassifying marijuana as a less dangerous drug, drawing objection from Rep. [[mike-lawler|Mike Lawler]], who called rescheduling "wrong" and described marijuana as a gateway drug.[146] Commentators additionally recalled Trump's recent threat to enter [[nigeria|Nigeria]] "guns-a-blazing" while discussing the [[ecowas|ECOWAS]] standoff over [[guinea-bissau|Guinea-Bissau]].[146] Meanwhile his [[justice-department|Justice Department]] pressed [[doj-voter-registration-list-lawsuits|18 lawsuits over state voter registration lists]], and Transportation Secretary [[sean-duffy|Sean Duffy]] advanced the administration's crackdown on [[commercial-drivers-licenses-for-immigrants|commercial driver's licenses for immigrants]].[146] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] characterized Trump's embrace of controlled chaos in the wake of trauma events such as the [[bondi-beach-mass-shooting|Bondi Beach mass shooting]] as "narrative disarmament."[146]

## 2024 Ballot, Damages, and the Border Deal (January 2024)

In late January 2024 Trump was simultaneously the presumptive Republican presidential nominee and the target of multiple legal actions. He publicly attacked the bipartisan Senate border-and-Ukraine package then under negotiation, characterizing the compromise as a political "gift" to Democrats, and his opposition was widely credited with helping to collapse Republican support for the deal.[147] House Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] echoed that resistance, suggesting the compromise would be "dead on arrival" in his chamber.[147]

On the legal front, the U.S. Supreme Court signaled that it was moving rapidly on Trump's appeal seeking to overturn the [[colorado-supreme-court|Colorado Supreme Court]] ruling that barred him from the state's ballot on Fourteenth Amendment grounds, scheduling oral argument for February 8, 2024 and indicating that written opinions would be announced the same day.[147] His attorneys' January 3 petition asked whether the Colorado court had incorrectly ordered him excluded from the 2024 presidential ballot.[147] Days earlier, a New York jury ordered Trump to pay $83.3 million in the second [[e-jean-carroll|E. Jean Carroll]] defamation case; he denounced the verdict on Truth Social as "absolutely ridiculous" and described the legal system as "out of control."[147]

Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed these proceedings through the phrase "Trump is the President of Precedent," arguing that each legal and narrative deployment against Trump was building the record for a broader confrontation, and that "they're not after him, they're after you — he's just in the way."[147]

## Snoop Dogg Reversal (January 2024)

The rapper [[snoop-dogg|Snoop Dogg]] said in a January 28, 2024 interview with The Times that he had "nothing but love and respect" for Trump, citing Trump's pardon of Michael Harris, co-founder of Snoop's first label Death Row Records.[147] Badlands writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] contrasted this with Snoop's 2017 "Lavender" video, in which he aimed a gun at a clown dressed as Trump, and argued that Trump-era policies such as the First Step Act, Opportunity Zones and the Platinum Plan had been misrepresented by the corporate press.[147]

## Acceptance of the 2024 GOP nomination

On the final evening of the [[2024-republican-national-convention|2024 Republican National Convention]], Thursday 18 July 2024, Trump formally accepted the Republican Party's presidential nomination, having abandoned his original 20-page speech earlier in the week following the [[attempted-assassination-of-donald-trump-2024|attempted assassination]] in Butler, Pennsylvania.[148] Saying "you'll never hear it from me a second time, because it's too painful to tell," he recounted turning to view an immigration chart when he heard a loud whizzing sound and felt something strike his right ear, bringing his hand down covered in blood before dropping to the ground.[148] He said the bullet fired by [[thomas-matthew-crooks|Thomas Matthew Crooks]] would have "perfectly hit its mark" had he not moved his head at the last instant, and told the convention that he stood before them "only by the grace of almighty God."[148] He praised rally attendees who did not stampede but stood in place and pointed toward the sniper, crediting them with saving lives.[148]

Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that in the aftermath Trump held the public mandate across formerly disparate theaters and could take his [[narrative-warfare|Narrative]] fighting style in any direction, from humbled statesman to righteous fury.[148] He observed that Trump's rhetoric had adopted a "grand unification theme" even by mainstream accounts, and that days after escaping death by a quarter inch Trump was greeted like a rock star at the convention with vanquished political foes bending the knee.[148] Burning Bright also noted polling indicating that over 30% of Democrats believed Trump had faked the shooting, describing this as an inversion of the conspiracy paradigm.[148]

Separately, the brief revisited Trump's 2018 diplomacy with [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]], recalling that he became the first U.S. president to enter [[north-korea|North Korea]] at the DMZ and pledged to help transform the country's coastline into a tourist destination, remarking of the beaches, "Boy, look at that view. Wouldn't that make a great condo?"[148]

## 2024 civil fraud judgment pause

On March 25, 2024, Trump won a bid to pause the $454 million civil fraud judgment against him, on the condition that he post a smaller $175 million bond within ten days; the ruling by a mid-level New York state appeals court blocked state authorities from taking steps to seize his assets.[149] Trump had said he would otherwise be forced to sell assets at "fire sale prices" to post bond, and the decision eased an acute cash crunch.[149] The ruling delayed enforcement of a judgment finding that he had overstated his wealth to dupe investors and lenders, without indicating how the appeals court might ultimately rule on the merits, a decision that could take a year or longer.[149] Speaking at a New York courthouse where he was appearing in a separate criminal case, Trump said he "greatly respect[ed] the decision of the appellate division" and would post "the $175 million in cash or bonds or securities or whatever is necessary, very quickly"; asked what collateral he would use, he answered "Cash."[149] The office of New York Attorney General [[letitia-james|Letitia James]] responded that Trump was "still facing accountability for his staggering fraud."[149] Badlands commentary framed the outcome as a boomerang against James and noted that the news broke the same day that [[trump-media-and-technology-group|TMTG]] was confirmed for public trading, an event estimated to raise Trump's personal wealth by roughly $6.5 billion.[149]

## July 2025: Powell, Vietnam and the "Big, Beautiful Bill"

On the evening of July 2, 2025, Trump amplified [[federal-housing-finance-agency|FHFA]] Director [[bill-pulte|Bill Pulte]]'s call for [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] Chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] to resign, consistent with his long-standing complaints about Powell's refusal to cut interest rates more quickly.[150]

The same day Trump announced via social media that the United States had reached a trade agreement with [[vietnam|Vietnam]] imposing a 20 per cent tariff on Vietnamese imports plus a 40 per cent duty on goods deemed transshipped, calling it "a great deal of cooperation between our two countries."[150] The rate was well below the 46 per cent "reciprocal" tariff he had announced in early April before pausing it for 90 days.[150]

Trump and House Republican leadership spent much of July 2 scrambling to shore up support for his massive domestic policy bill, the [[one-big-beautiful-bill-act|One Big Beautiful Bill]], ahead of a self-imposed July 4 deadline to get the measure — which had squeaked through the [[us-senate|Senate]] on July 1 — to his desk.[150] Badlands contributor [[chris-paul|Chris Paul]] argued that no member of [[congress|Congress]] enjoys anything like Trump's mandate to govern, that most members are unknown even in their own districts and cannot prove their legitimacy because "our elections are fake," and that all 435 House members should be backing the bill.[150] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] offered a different reading, contending that Trump "isn't weathering attacks on his agenda" but "provoking and then transforming them," since lasting constitutional change would genuinely require challenges to the President.[150]

## Tariff threats on China, February 2024

In an interview broadcast Sunday, February 4, 2024 on Fox's "Sunday Morning Futures," Trump confirmed that he was considering imposing tariffs of 60% or higher on Chinese goods in a potential second term, escalating the [[us-china-trade-war-2025|US–China trade war]] he launched during his first term.[151] "We have to do it," he said, adding that he might go higher still: "Maybe it's going to be more than that."[151] The Washington Post had first reported that his campaign was weighing a theoretical 60% Chinese tariff plan.[151] Beyond China, Trump had said he would impose a blanket 10% tariff on all US imports, a proposal criticized by his sole remaining primary challenger [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]] for its effect on American consumers.[151]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] cited the episode as an example of Trump's ability to steer the news cycle through narrative deployments alone, noting that his statements sent American investments in Chinese markets cratering and did nothing to slow the crisis cascade in Chinese stocks.[151] Under the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]] framing, Burning Bright argued, such tariffs function less as threats than as catalysts for the one trend benefiting both nations — boosting domestic production as the superpowers retreat inward to preserve strength and independence.[151] The same commentator separately argued that Trump had executed a "bluff charge" at the [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] that week, predicting rate cuts intended to manufacture a faux economic rebound ahead of the election.[151]

## September 2025: designations, vetoes, and confirmations

On 17 September 2025, Trump announced that [[antifa|Antifa]] would be designated a "major terrorist organization" in the United States following the assassination of conservative activist [[charlie-kirk|Charlie Kirk]]; the Dutch parliament adopted a parallel motion from [[geert-wilders|Geert Wilders]] the next day.[152] Badlands contributor Burning Bright noted that Trump had first signaled the designation in 2020 and argued he was cultivating mandate on the back of the assassination rather than reacting to it.[152] The same day, the [[capital-research-center|Capital Research Center]] — whose board includes [[edwin-meese-iii|Edwin Meese III]], recipient of the Presidential Medal of Freedom from Trump in 2019 — published a report alleging that the [[open-society-foundations|Open Society Foundations]] had funded groups tied to terrorism, material commentators viewed as a possible predicate for RICO charges against [[george-soros|George Soros]].[152]

Speaking to reporters during a trip to the United Kingdom on 18 September 2025, Trump renewed his attacks on the [[autopen-scandal|autopen]] pardons of former President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], saying the device was "illegally used" and that Biden "never gave the orders."[152] That same day, Senate Republicans used the [[nuclear-option|nuclear option]] rules change to confirm 48 of Trump's nominees en bloc along party lines, and the United States — through representative [[morgan-ortagus|Morgan Ortagus]] — cast its sixth veto of a Gaza ceasefire resolution at the [[united-nations-security-council|UN Security Council]].[152] Trump had by then been in office exactly eight months.[152]

### Memphis deployment, Antifa designation talk and Venezuela (September 2025)

On Monday, 15 September 2025, Trump signed an executive order extending federal law enforcement action to [[memphis|Memphis]], Tennessee, deploying [[national-guard|National Guard]] troops and creating the [[memphis-state-task-force|Memphis Safe Task Force]] — an interagency body including the [[fbi|FBI]], [[drug-enforcement-administration|DEA]], [[immigration-and-customs-enforcement|ICE]] and the [[us-marshals-service|U.S. Marshals Service]] — which he called a "replica" of his August crackdown in Washington, D.C.[154] He cited "tremendous levels of violent crime that have overwhelmed its local government's ability to respond effectively," though police reported offences at a 25-year low, and he had pressed for similar deployments in Baltimore and Chicago.[154] Tennessee governor [[bill-lee|Bill Lee]] appeared with him at the White House signing ceremony while the Memphis mayor said he had not asked for the Guard.[154]

The same day, Trump said he would consider designating [[antifa|Antifa]] and other left-wing groups as domestic terrorists, saying "It's something I would do, yeah," conditional on support from Cabinet officials and the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]].[154] Badlands contributor [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] recalled the memo Trump issued on 5 January 2021 ordering an assessment of whether to classify Antifa as a terrorist organization — the day before what Badlands describes as the J6 false flag.[154]

Trump also declined to rule out striking mainland [[venezuela|Venezuela]], telling reporters in Morristown, New Jersey, "We'll see what happens... Venezuela is sending us their gang members, their drug dealers, and drugs. It's not acceptable."[154] Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued instead that Trump had been working with [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolás Maduro]] to bring a transnational corporate cartel to heel through deception and misdirection.[154] On trade, Trump pressed the [[g7|G7]] and the [[european-union|EU]] for secondary tariffs of up to 100% on Chinese and Indian goods tied to Russian oil, drawing a rebuke from Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson [[lin-jian|Lin Jian]].[154] He further proposed on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that U.S. companies move from quarterly to semiannual reporting, a change that would require [[securities-and-exchange-commission|SEC]] approval.[154] A week before Secretary of State [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]]'s Jerusalem visit, Trump had chided [[israel|Israel]] for air strikes on Hamas leaders in the U.S. partner state of Qatar.[154]

### Putin's commentary on Trump's prosecutions (September 2023)

Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] used remarks before the Eastern Economic Forum in Vladivostok in September 2023 to mock the United States over the prosecutions of Trump, arguing that the cases exposed the "rottenness of the American political system, which cannot pretend to teach others democracy."[153] Putin described the proceedings as "the persecution of a political rival for political reasons," conducted openly before the American and world publics.[153] American conservative commentators had already characterised the prosecutions as befitting a "banana republic," and Badlands Media writers argued that the combination of an internationally visible lawfare campaign and escalating great-power confrontation was building a public mandate for "problem-solvers and peacemakers."[153] Separately, [[kari-lake|Kari Lake]] was described in the same brief as one of the few American political figures consistently and strongly supported by Trump.[153]

## October 2023: border warning and Georgia case developments

Days before the October 2023 Lewiston, Maine mass shooting, Trump warned that the Biden administration's handling of the US border could pave the way for fresh attacks from within the country — a warning [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] cited in the [[badlands-news-brief|Badlands News Brief]] as one reason the timing of the attack appeared suspect.[155] In the same period, his former attorney [[jenna-ellis|Jenna Ellis]] pleaded guilty in the Georgia racketeering case to aiding and abetting false statements in writing, a plea that commentators including [[jonathan-turley|Jonathan Turley]] said carried greater risk for Trump than earlier pleas because it could implicate him if Ellis asserted he knew the statements were false; Ellis had recently broken with him publicly, calling him a "malignant narcissist."[155] Also in October 2023, Trump ally [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] — who in 2020 had solicited House Republican signatures for an amicus brief supporting the Texas suit to invalidate electoral college votes — was elected Speaker of the House.[155]

## August 2023 indictment and the GOP debate meme

Trump was indicted for a third time on August 1, 2023, over alleged efforts to overturn the 2020 election, and he pre-announced the news himself on [[truth-social|Truth Social]] that morning, writing that "Deranged Jack Smith, in order to interfere with the Presidential Election of 2024, will be putting out yet another Fake Indictment of your favorite President, me, at 5:00 P.M."[156] He framed the timing as deliberate — "Why didn't they do this 2.5 years ago? ... Because they wanted to put it right in the middle of my campaign" — and called it "Prosecutorial Misconduct!"[156] Florida Governor [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]] was among the notable defenders, pledging to end the weaponization of government and replace the FBI Director if elected.[156]

Days earlier, on July 31, 2023, Trump had teased that the [[first-gop-presidential-debate-2023|first Republican primary debate]] might help him choose a running mate, posting "Let them debate so I can see who I MIGHT consider for Vice President!"[156] Leading the primary field, he had not decided whether to participate, and his stance had not shifted by the following day.[156] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the post as Trump converting the debates into a meme under his own control, elevating himself and the [[america-first-movement|America First movement]] while denigrating the GOP and the two-party system.[156]

## Epstein files, Bondi, and Russiagate (July 2025)

In July 2025 Trump defended Attorney General [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]] amid growing calls from his own supporters for her resignation over a [[justice-department|Justice Department]] memo that failed to deliver expected revelations in the [[epstein-files|Epstein]] case.[157] In a lengthy social media post he hit out at critics who accused Bondi of withholding information about [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]]'s death and his so-called client list, writing in all caps, "Let Pam Bondi do her job," and encouraging supporters not to "waste time and energy" on Epstein.[157] Badlands host [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] cautioned that people were willing to lie in service of an assumed plan, while [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] contrasted the episode with an earlier Trump clip on Epstein and asked why legacy media focused only on the later one.[157]

The same week, CIA Director [[john-ratcliffe|John Ratcliffe]] made a criminal referral concerning former CIA chief [[john-brennan|John Brennan]] to FBI Director [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]], leaving the charging decision with Bondi.[157] Badlands writer [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] rejected the claim that the administration was amplifying [[russiagate-hoax|Russiagate]] to distract from Epstein, noting that Trump had never retracted his statement that the conspirators were all caught.[157]

## Russia statement and sanctions signaling

On Friday, July 11, 2025, Trump said he intended to deliver a "major statement" about Russia early the following week, telling NBC News, "I think I'll have a major statement to make on Russia on Monday."[157] Speaking on the White House lawn the same morning, after an overnight Russian drone attack damaged a hospital in Kharkiv, Ukraine, he remarked, "You'll be seeing things happen."[157] As the Senate weighed legislation to compel action on sanctions, the president signaled willingness to escalate measures against Moscow, including the possibility of targeting buyers of Russian oil, minerals and other commodities.[157] Burning Bright framed the pressure on Trump as evidence that "the Sovereign Alliance is real" and winning.[157]

## Powell pressure and the planning commission

Trump also quietly replaced a number of Biden-era appointees on the [[national-capital-planning-commission|National Capital Planning Commission]] with figures from his [[white-house|White House]], a maneuver reported as an effort to oust [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] Chairman [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]] or force him to cut interest rates by leveraging the commission's authority over government buildings, including the Fed's headquarters.[157]

## July 2025: Netanyahu talks, tariff revenue, and the Epstein memo

On July 8, 2025, Trump hosted [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] for a second White House meeting in as many days, joined by Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]], without any public announcement of a breakthrough in the Gaza ceasefire and hostage release talks that were the primary topic.[158] Earlier the same day Trump and Mideast envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] met a delegation of senior Qatari officials at the White House for more than three hours.[158] Badlands commentary held that the visits had been engineered by [[ron-dermer|Ron Dermer]] following Trump's public remark that Israel and Iran "don't know what the f— they are doing," and read Trump's decision to relegate the first meeting to an after-hours dinner as a signal of displeasure with Israeli pressure to support annexation of Palestine.[158]

The same week, Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] credited Trump's aggressive trade policy with roughly $100 billion in tariff revenue collected in 2025 and projected as much as $300 billion by December, while the [[us-department-of-the-treasury|Treasury]] moved to rebuild its cash balance following passage of Trump's $5 trillion tax and spending bill with its accompanying debt ceiling hike.[158]

Trump also publicly dismissed continuing questions about [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]] after the release of a joint Justice Department and [[fbi|FBI]] memo concluding that Epstein died by suicide and possessed no client list.[158] "Are you still talking about Jeffrey Epstein? This guy's been talked about for years," he said in response to a question directed at Attorney General [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]], adding that raising the subject amid the Texas flood tragedy "just seems like a desecration."[158] Separately, CBS management settled a lawsuit Trump had filed in order to remain in the administration's good graces while seeking FCC approval of the Skydance–Paramount merger.[158] See [[doj-fbi-epstein-memo|DOJ-FBI Epstein Memo]].[158]

## UFC 295 appearance (November 2023)

On Saturday, November 11, 2023, Trump entered Madison Square Garden for [[ufc|UFC]] 295 to a loud ovation from the crowd, flanked by an entourage of [[kid-rock|Kid Rock]], [[tucker-carlson|Tucker Carlson]], UFC president [[dana-white|Dana White]] and son [[donald-trump-jr|Don Jr.]].[159] A well-known boxing and mixed martial arts fan, the 45th president arrived moments before the highly anticipated pay-per-view event began, pumping his fist and waving to the sold-out arena as Kid Rock's "American Badass" played, before taking his ringside seat.[159]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump's UFC appearances carry political weight beyond the anecdotal, noting that [[john-mccain|John McCain]] had attempted to shut the UFC down in the early 2000s by pressuring New York State against the league, and that Trump had once offered the promotion a free venue and transportation to save a last-minute event whose cancellation might have bankrupted both the company and the emerging sport.[159] He described the UFC as the counter-culture sport of the new century and contended that Trump timed his 2023 appearances to coincide with major narrative deployments, producing clips that capture his appeal to the American everyman and the counter-culture alike — "Politics IS Culture. Culture IS Politics."[159]

## Public opinion on the June 2023 indictment

A Harvard/Harris poll published in June 2023 found that 55 percent of Americans believed Trump's federal indictment was politically motivated and 56 percent regarded it as election interference, while only 44 percent saw it as "the fair application of the law."[160] Among Republicans, 83 percent viewed the indictment as a political exercise, as did 55 percent of independents, while support was concentrated among Democrats at roughly 80 percent.[160] The same survey found that 65 percent believed [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] had mishandled classified material and 72 percent said the same of [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]]'s email scandal.[160] See [[harvard-harris-poll-on-the-trump-indictment|Harvard/Harris poll on the Trump indictment]].[160]

In the same week, the [[durham-report|Durham report]] returned to prominence ahead of two congressional hearings, having concluded that the [[fbi|FBI]] and [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] had no proper basis to investigate alleged ties between Trump's 2016 campaign and Russia and that no actual evidence of collusion existed when [[crossfire-hurricane|Crossfire Hurricane]] began.[160] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued that four years of baseless Russia claims had obstructed Trump's ability to deliver for Americans, describing the conduct of the bureaucratic state as closer to treason than to any ordinary offense.[160]

## SCIF dispute in the classified documents case (August 2023)

In August 2023 Trump's attorneys asked the court to set up a [[sensitive-compartmented-information-facility|sensitive compartmented information facility (SCIF)]] so that the former president could discuss classified material with his defense team; prosecutors for special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] objected on August 14, calling it "special treatment that no other criminal defendant would receive."[161] The filing before Judge [[aileen-cannon|Aileen Cannon]] also accused Trump of misrepresenting the protective order issued by Judge [[tanya-chutkan|Tanya Chutkan]].[161] Separately, [[hillary-clinton|Hillary Clinton]] told MSNBC's [[rachel-maddow|Rachel Maddow]] that the indictments were a "terrible moment" for America while suggesting "the only satisfaction may be that the system is working."[161] A RealClearPolitics essay aggregated in the same brief cast Trump's refusal to accept the 2020 result as an act of individualist defiance against a system he had reason to distrust after five years of attacks, and noted that his enemies had brought three indictments with a fourth expected as he mounted a comeback bid.[161]

## May 2025 news cycle

In a *Meet the Press* interview aired in early May 2025, Trump discussed ongoing peace negotiations between Russia and Ukraine and how long he planned to give both countries before walking away from an agreement, saying "maybe it's not possible" to reach a deal.[162] Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump would be known as "the Peacemaker of our time" and predicted a total inversion of the post-WW2 Axis and Allies paradigm.[162]

During an Oval Office meeting with Canadian Prime Minister [[mark-carney|Mark Carney]], Trump announced that the United States would stop bombing the [[houthis|Houthis]] in Yemen after claiming the group had agreed to stop interrupting Middle East shipping lanes, an announcement made without advance notice to Israel.[162] He also teased a major forthcoming announcement tied to his upcoming Middle East trip.[162] Two days before the Carney meeting, Trump was asked by the host of *Meet the Press* whether he would consider using military force against Canada; he said only that he thought it was "highly unlikely."[162]

The [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] allowed his administration to enforce the [[transgender-military-ban|ban on transgender people in the military]] while legal challenges proceeded.[162] His pick for U.S. attorney for the District of Columbia, [[ed-martin|Ed Martin]], suffered a likely fatal blow when Senator [[thom-tillis|Thom Tillis]] announced his opposition.[162] The hacker collective [[anonymous|Anonymous]] defaced the website of [[globalx|GlobalX]], the deportation carrier, calling Trump a "fascist."[162]

## July 2025: Ukraine ultimatum, Education ruling, and Epstein backlash

On July 14, 2025, hosting NATO Secretary General [[mark-rutte|Mark Rutte]] at the [[white-house|White House]], Trump gave Russia 50 days to resolve its war on [[ukraine|Ukraine]] or face "very severe" secondary tariffs of about 100% targeting Moscow's remaining trade partners, telling reporters "we're very, very unhappy" with Russia.[163] The two simultaneously unveiled a deal under which [[nato|NATO]] would buy American weapons — including Patriot anti-missile batteries — for distribution to Ukraine, bankrolled by European members and coordinated by envoy [[matthew-whitaker|Matthew Whitaker]].[163] Retired French general [[francois-chauvancy|Francois Chauvancy]] doubted the alliance's capacity to execute the plan and said Trump was "primarily aiming for publicity."[163] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] instead read the arrangement as Trump deliberately bankrupting NATO, while [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] speculated that Trump and [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] have long coordinated as part of the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]].[163]

The same day the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] cleared the way for his administration to massively reduce the [[department-of-education|Department of Education]], pausing a district-court reinstatement order over a dissent by Justice [[sonia-sotomayor|Sonia Sotomayor]].[163] Meanwhile, the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]]'s memo declaring that no [[jeffrey-epstein|Epstein]] client list exists triggered outrage in his base, with [[steve-bannon|Steve Bannon]] warning of a 40-seat House loss in 2026, and the [[pentagon|Pentagon]] both awarded [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s [[xai|xAI]] a [[grok|Grok]] contract and withdrew from the [[aspen-security-forum|Aspen Security Forum]].[163]

## January 6, 2025: certification, courts, and city states

On January 6, 2025, [[congress|Congress]] certified Trump's election victory, sealing a political comeback described as a full 180-degree turn from four years earlier, when he sat in the [[white-house|White House]] as his supporters stormed the Capitol in an attempt to halt certification of his defeat to [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[164] Over the intervening four years he had sought to rewrite the events of January 6, 2021, calling it a "day of love," referring to those arrested as "hostages," and vowing to pardon them immediately upon taking office; the certification is treated in detail at [[2024-election-certification|the 2024 election certification]].[164] The same day the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] disclosed that as many as 200 more [[january-6-prosecutions|January 6 cases]] remained to be charged, a figure characterised as a signal flare to Trump.[164]

Also on January 6, his lawyers [[todd-blanche|Todd Blanche]] and [[emil-bove|Emil Bove]] failed to persuade Justice [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]] to pause that week's sentencing in the [[trump-hush-money-case|hush money case]], which had been set for January 10, ten days before Inauguration Day.[164]

In the same period [[pope-francis|Pope Francis]] appointed Trump critic [[robert-mcelroy|Robert McElroy]] as archbishop of Washington, D.C., shortly after Trump tapped Francis critic [[brian-burch|Brian Burch]] of [[catholicvote|CatholicVote]] as his ambassador to the Vatican — a juxtaposition [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] read as part of a "city state" power shift spanning Washington, the City of London, and Vatican City.[164] Trump separately blasted the [[washington-post|Washington Post]] on Truth Social over a report that aides were paring back his [[trump-universal-tariff-plan|universal tariff plan]], calling it "Fake News" quoting "so-called anonymous sources, which don't exist"; the dollar reversed a 1% intraday drop after his denial.[164] He also pledged to reverse [[biden-offshore-drilling-ban|Biden's offshore drilling ban]] immediately upon taking office.[164]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that Trump's "Narrative Deployments" regarding Canada had seismic real-world effects, noting his shift from describing the 51st state as a trollish hypothetical to saying many people love "BEING the 51st State," and framing [[2025-trudeau-resignation|Justin Trudeau's resignation]] as a demonstration to the globalist class that its prized puppets were now marching to the beat of patriot drums.[164]

## Crypto outreach and Capitol Hill agenda-setting (June 2024)

On the night of Tuesday, June 11, 2024, Trump met several [[bitcoin|Bitcoin]] miners at Mar-a-Lago, telling them he loves and understands cryptocurrency and that miners help stabilize energy supply from the grid, and pledging to be an advocate for miners in the White House.[165] Attendees included CleanSpark executive chairman Matthew Schultz and Riot Platforms chief executive Jason Les.[165] Trump had increasingly highlighted digital assets on the campaign trail as a way to reach new voters, taking advice from [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]], pledging at a Libertarian Party convention to commute the sentence of Silk Road founder Ross Ulbricht, and accepting crypto donations.[165]

Separately, Axios reported that Trump would huddle with the House Republican Conference at the Capitol Hill Club at 9:30am on Thursday, June 13, during a Washington visit that also included a meeting with Senate Republicans on a "strategic governing agenda in 2025" and an appearance before corporate executives at a Business Roundtable event.[165] The trip came as Trump plotted with House Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] to hit the ground running with a Republican policy agenda if he retook the White House in November.[165]

Badlands commentary on the same day argued that Trump, as the principal narrative-setter for the patriot side of the Info War, could appear bipartisan by defending [[hunter-biden|Hunter Biden]]'s constitutional rights after the [[hunter-biden-gun-conviction|gun conviction]], using both men's prosecutions as evidence of a weaponized justice system.[165]

## September 2024 debate challenge

On Friday, August 2, 2024, Trump announced on Truth Social that he had agreed with [[fox-news|Fox News]] to debate [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] on Wednesday, September 4, in Pennsylvania, moderated by [[bret-baier|Bret Baier]] and [[martha-maccallum|Martha MacCallum]], under rules similar to his earlier debate with [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] but "WITH A FULL ARENA AUDIENCE."[166] He said the previously scheduled ABC debate had been terminated because Biden would no longer participate and because he was in litigation against the network.[166] Trump wrote that Democrats had "Unconstitutionally taken a Candidate, who was acknowledged to be defeated, and unceremoniously replaced him," calling it a threat to democracy while saying he was "totally prepared to accept the results of this 'coup.'"[166] He noted the date fell just before the September 6 start of early voting.[166]

Badlands commentator [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] described the debate as one of several traps the establishment had been baited into, alongside the resurfaced birth-certificate controversy that returned [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]] and presidential eligibility to the news cycle, and Trump's remarks on Harris's racial identity before a gathering of Black journalists.[166]

## Positioning on Israel and the September 11 case

[[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued that Trump has been maneuvering to isolate [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] within Israeli politics and internationally — a strategy he called "Trump's Gambit" — citing Trump's courtship of centrists such as [[benny-gantz|Benny Gantz]] and speculating about accelerationist messaging delivered at their Mar-a-Lago meeting.[166] The same commentary held that Netanyahu's conduct in the final days of Trump's first term, including the [[ron-dermer|Ron Dermer]] reversal of sanctions on [[dan-gertler|Dan Gertler]] through the [[state-department|State Department]] and his early congratulatory call to Biden, helped make [[devolution|Devolution]] necessary.[166] It also recalled Trump's 2019 endorsement of Turkish President [[recep-tayyip-erdogan|Recep Tayyip Erdoğan]], whom he described himself as a "big fan" of.[166]

Separately, Burning Bright argued that the [[guantanamo-bay|Guantánamo]] plea deal for [[khalid-sheikh-mohammed|Khalid Sheikh Mohammed]] and his co-defendants had been weaponized by Trump and allied figures to portray the incumbent regime as friendly to accused terrorists while cultivating a public mandate to pursue the full September 11 story.[166]

## October 2024: podcasts, policy adoption and the Info War
In late October 2024, a campaign official confirmed that Trump would record an interview with Joe Rogan at the podcaster's Austin, Texas studio, giving him access to the largest podcast audience in the United States and a further opportunity to court younger male voters.[167] Trump had already appeared on shows including Barstool Sports' "Bussin with the Boys," "Full Send," and Patrick Bet-David's podcast.[167]

Badlands read the same news cycle as evidence that Trump's policies had become electorally dominant: Senate Democrats in "blue wall" states were distancing themselves from Kamala Harris, and Pennsylvania's Bob Casey went so far as to tout having "sided with Trump to end NAFTA" and to embrace Trump's tariff policies — a proof point, in the site's framing, that "we are not a 50/50 country."[167]

Commentary also revisited Trump's June 2023 statement that "this persecution is being done by the same weaponized agencies that for seven years have been running illegal psychological warfare campaigns against the American people, much as if they were trying to destabilize a foreign country," noting that he named specific operations: the "Russia Russia Russia!" hoax, the Mueller "no collusion" witch hunt, the two impeachment hoaxes, and the 51 lying intelligence officers.[167] Separately, Badlands writer Burning Bright predicted Trump would ultimately be known as the peacemaker who does not sue for peace but demands it, describing him as the awaited third leg of a tripod alongside Vladimir Putin and Xi Jinping.[167]

## Carter funeral and Greenland remarks (January 2025)

As president-elect, Trump attended the [[state-funeral-of-jimmy-carter|state funeral of Jimmy Carter]] on January 9, 2025, at the Washington National Cathedral, joining the four other living presidents despite having long been an outsider among them and having previously skipped the 2020 services for Representative John Lewis, the 2018 services for Senator [[john-mccain|John McCain]] to which he was not invited, and the 2018 funeral of Barbara Bush.[168] With [[michelle-obama|Michelle Obama]] absent in Hawaii, [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]] was seated beside him, and video footage showed the two smiling and laughing before the service began.[168]

The same week, Trump refused to rule out annexing [[greenland|Greenland]] for national security and economic purposes, drawing a Kremlin call for Arctic stability from spokesman [[dmitry-peskov|Dmitry Peskov]] and warnings from analysts that any use of force against a Danish territory would implicate NATO's Article 5.[168] His transition spokesperson [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] said every decision the president-elect makes "is in the best interest of the United States and the American people."[168] Podcaster [[joe-rogan|Joe Rogan]] separately floated annexing Mexico after Canada, echoing ideas Trump had suggested his administration could pursue.[168]

In the same period Trump was reported to have called Justice [[samuel-alito|Samuel Alito]] about the qualifications of Alito's former clerk William Levi for a government position, prompting extensive media coverage that critics said ignored the standard practice of judges providing references for former clerks.[168] Trump also drew [[marco-rubio|Marco Rubio]] into his administration as Secretary of State, creating the Florida Senate vacancy that Governor [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]] was expected to fill with Attorney General [[ashley-moody|Ashley Moody]].[168]

## October 2023: New York civil fraud trial and the Bowman affair

On the evening of October 1, 2023, Trump announced on Truth Social that he would appear in court the following morning for the start of his [[new-york-civil-fraud-trial|New York civil fraud trial]], writing, "I'm going to Court tomorrow morning to fight for my name and reputation."[169] Law enforcement and court employees in Lower Manhattan had already been preparing security for his expected appearance on Monday and possibly Tuesday.[169] His plans had become clearer after a federal judge in Florida granted his request to postpone a deposition in a separate matter that conflicted with the New York trial's start.[169] Trump flew to New York City on Sunday evening following a campaign event in Ottumwa, Iowa, and stayed the night at Trump Tower; the case, brought in September 2022 by Attorney General [[letitia-james|Letitia James]] against Trump, his eldest sons, their companies and several executives, was set to begin at 10 a.m. Monday.[169]

The same weekend, Trump called Representative [[jamaal-bowman|Jamaal Bowman]]'s conduct in the [[2023-cannon-building-fire-alarm-incident|Cannon Building fire alarm incident]] egregious.[169] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] answered the "walls are closing in" framing by arguing that it was [[joe-biden|Biden]] on trial — in Congress and in the court of public opinion — and that the [[media-industrial-complex|Media Industrial Complex]] and establishment politicians on both sides of an "illusory aisle" would rally to defend the Biden Crime Family, the [[uniparty|Uniparty]] establishment, and the [[deep-state|Deep State]].[169] He closed with the line that [[barack-obama|Obama]] promised "Hope and Change" while "Donald Trump is going to bring it."[169]

In congressional politics that week, Burning Bright also described the clash between Speaker [[kevin-mccarthy|Kevin McCarthy]] and Representative [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]] as [[kayfabe|kayfabe]], asserting that both men were Trump's choices — one for Speaker, the other for opposition to that Speaker.[169]

## Iranian assassination threat briefing (September 2024)
