That same week, Washington prepared for the [[2025-army-250th-birthday-military-parade|military parade]] honoring the 250th birthday of the [[united-states-army|Army]] and Trump's 79th birthday, with more than 18 miles of anti-scale fencing and 175 magnetometers deployed by the [[united-states-secret-service|Secret Service]].[208] Trump also faced renewed [[epstein-files|Epstein]] allegations after [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] claimed on X that he was named in the files; Epstein's former attorney [[david-schoen|David Schoen]] said "authoritatively, unequivocally, and definitely" that Epstein "had no information to hurt President Trump."[208] On trade, Trump said negotiations with [[china|China]] in London were "not easy" but that he was "only getting good reports," with Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] and Commerce Secretary [[howard-lutnick|Howard Lutnick]] calling the talks good and fruitful.[208]

## June 2024 debate approach and polling coverage

While [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] spent the week of June 20, 2024 at [[camp-david|Camp David]] in intensive debate preparation led by [[ron-klain|Ron Klain]], Trump approached the June 27 CNN debate in Atlanta as just another campaign appearance, publicly dismissing the notion that he needed anything resembling his opponent's all-hands-on-deck effort.[210]

The same week, [[newsweek|Newsweek]] reported that Trump had suffered an "abysmal polling week," citing three surveys showing him behind Biden, including a Fox News poll of 1,095 registered voters giving Biden 50 percent to Trump's 48 — a three-point shift from May and the first Fox lead for Biden since October.[210] [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] commentator [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] countered that the piece buried an admission that other polls showed Trump leading in its eleventh paragraph, that Newsweek had contacted the Trump campaign outside business hours, and that the race was not a dead heat but one in which Trump was up in every demographic.[210]

Badlands commentary also invoked Trump's 2017 foreign tour as the template that [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]]'s June 2024 visits to [[north-korea|North Korea]] and [[vietnam|Vietnam]] were said to mirror, and credited Trump with cutting the strings that had made [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]] a [[cia|CIA]] puppet.[210] Separately, the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] was preparing to rule on Trump's claim of "absolute immunity" from criminal prosecution as part of its end-of-term docket.[210]

## July 2023 January 6 target letter and Republican rally

On July 18, 2023, Trump announced on Truth Social that Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] had sent him a letter the previous Sunday night designating him a target of the January 6 grand jury investigation and giving him four days to appear, which he said almost always means arrest and indictment.[210] Trump called Smith "deranged," described the probe as the work of "Joe Biden's DOJ," and said the fight had been never-ending "since the day I came down the escalator in Trump Tower."[210] He characterized the investigation as election interference and "a complete and total political weaponization of law enforcement."[210]

Top Republicans publicly defended him, with [[kevin-mccarthy|Kevin McCarthy]], [[steve-scalise|Steve Scalise]] and [[marjorie-taylor-greene|Marjorie Taylor Greene]] all framing the action as a double standard relative to the handling of the [[hunter-biden|Hunter Biden]] investigation.[210] Badlands commentary observed that the prospective third indictment of the year appeared to be strengthening rather than weakening Trump's support, casting him as an anti-establishment underdog even among figures normally considered part of the establishment.[210]

Separately, commentary that month on a possible second Trump term — prompted by former chief of staff [[john-kelly|John Kelly]]'s prediction of a "non-stop gunfight" with [[congress|Congress]] — argued that a term-limited Trump, freed from re-election concerns, could pursue political retribution, seal the border, end support for Ukraine, choose a MAGA successor, and make history in a manner exceeding [[grover-cleveland|Grover Cleveland]]'s non-consecutive return to the White House.[210] Straw polling among younger Republican voters at the time continued to show Trump as the party's clear front-runner for 2024.[210]

## November 2025: Epstein files, Saudi F-35s, and the Gaza resolution

In November 2025, President Donald Trump urged House lawmakers to vote for a measure forcing the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]] to release all federal records on [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]], saying they should do so "because we have nothing to hide," and pledged to sign the bill immediately after Senate passage: "Let the Senate look at it. Let anybody look at it," he told reporters in the Oval Office.[211] Badlands contributor [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] observed that because the DOJ is part of the executive branch, Trump could simply order the release himself, and concluded that he wanted the public spectacle.[211] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued in an essay titled "The Temple Trap" that the Epstein narrative had originally been meant as a weapon against Trump before he turned it back on his opponents.[211]

The same day, Trump said he would approve the sale of [[f-35|F-35]] fighter jets to [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], making the kingdom the first Middle Eastern state other than [[israel|Israel]] to obtain them, ahead of a White House meeting with Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]] covering the jets, a U.S.–Saudi security agreement, and possible Saudi normalization with Israel.[211] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read Trump's willingness to proceed despite Israel's [[qualitative-military-edge|qualitative military edge]] as a major sign of his allegiance to the Arab states over Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu.[211]

Also in November 2025, the [[united-nations-security-council|UN Security Council]] approved a U.S.-led resolution establishing an [[international-stabilization-force|international stabilization force]] for [[gaza|Gaza]] and endorsing the [[board-of-peace|Board of Peace]] that Trump has said he will chair, lending his peace plan additional momentum and international legitimacy.[211] Separately, the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] agreed to hear a border-crossings case in which the Trump administration argued that a Ninth Circuit ruling had already caused "untold interference with the Executive Branch's ability to manage the southern border."[211]

## Reported second-term plans against Mexican cartels (May 2024)

In May 2024, [[rolling-stone|Rolling Stone]] reported that if Trump won a second term he wanted to covertly deploy American assassination squads into Mexico soon after being sworn in, according to three people who had discussed the matter with him.[212] The report noted that both during and after his presidency he had floated bombing or invading Mexico in response to the American [[fentanyl-crisis|fentanyl crisis]] and to "wage WAR" on [[mexican-drug-cartels|drug cartels]], and cited former defense secretary [[mark-esper|Mark Esper]] as saying Trump had believed it possible to bomb cartel drug labs and pin the strikes on another country.[212] Trump was at the time campaigning on a public vow to "make appropriate use of Special Forces, cyber warfare, and other overt and covert actions to inflict maximum damage on cartel leadership, infrastructure, and operations."[212] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the piece functioned as free campaign material, since it warned Americans about plans to eliminate cartel influence, organized crime and a fentanyl crisis killing tens of thousands annually.[212]

In the same period Trump confirmed to reporters outside the courthouse that his lawyers had filed what he called a "major motion" in the New York appellate division challenging the gag order in his ongoing criminal trial, saying he was "essentially not allowed to talk to you about anything meaningful that's going on in the case."[212] Separately, attorney [[adam-leitman-bailey|Adam Leitman Bailey]] disclosed that he had approached Justice [[arthur-engoron|Arthur Engoron]] with unsolicited advice weeks before the judge fined Trump $454 million in the [[new-york-civil-fraud-trial|New York civil fraud case]], prompting a judicial-conduct investigation.[212]

## Harvard CAPS/Harris favorability (July 2023)

The [[harvard-harris-poll|Harvard CAPS/Harris]] poll released in late July 2023 put Trump's favorability at an unchanged 45 percent, second to [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]] at 47 percent, and showed him defeating his Republican challengers comfortably while leading [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] by five points and [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] by nine.[213]


## Powell Feud, Easter Messaging and Border Powers (April 2025)

Through April 2025 Trump escalated a public feud with [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] Chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]], whom he mocked as "Mr. Too Late," and prepared for a possible firing well before Powell's term expired in May 2026.[214] After [[kevin-hassett|Kevin Hassett]] confirmed the administration was still studying removal options, the dollar index fell to a three-year low while gold set a record and [[bitcoin|Bitcoin]] spiked.[214] Badlands commentary framed the confrontation as a deliberate attempt to "provoke and disclose," arguing Trump would obtain his "preemptive cuts" while tariffs, tax breaks and onshoring created an offsetting deflationary cycle and a shift toward sound money.[214] On the same weekend Trump celebrated Easter at the White House with a sarcastic post again suggesting Joe Biden had not been the real president, the White House replaced the COVID content at covid.gov with a page on the lab-leak theory, and Trump ordered flags lowered worldwide after the death of [[pope-francis|Pope Francis]].[214] His administration also weighed invoking the [[insurrection-act|Insurrection Act of 1807]] at the southern border, threatened military action against [[iran|Iran]] over its nuclear program while signalling a deal permitting civil nuclear energy, and faced [[harvard-university|Harvard]]'s lawsuit over frozen federal research funding.[214] The Atlantic reported that his 145 percent tariffs on Chinese goods had throttled supply chains in Yiwu, the world's largest wholesale market, where factory workers responded with defiance — "You think we're afraid of America?" — and little appetite for détente.[214]

## Freedom 250 celebration announcement (December 2025)

On Thursday, December 18, 2025, Trump outlined the festivities the [[white-house|White House]] is planning to commemorate the nation's 250th birthday, making good on his pledge to give America "the most spectacular birthday party the world has ever seen."[215] Having repeatedly promised on the 2024 campaign trail a grand celebration comparable to past world's fairs, he officially unveiled the list of events, called "Freedom 250," on social media.[215] Several activities are planned from June 25 to July 10, including a "Spirit of America" parade honoring fallen soldiers, a "Birthday Fireworks Celebration" on the National Mall billed as the largest such display in the world, and a "Great American State Fair" featuring pavilions from all 50 states.[215]

## Fusion, cannabis and foreign-asset developments of December 18, 2025

The same day, [[trump-media-and-technology-group|Trump Media & Technology Group]] announced a $6 billion all-stock merger with fusion developer [[tae-technologies|TAE Technologies]], a transaction Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read as evidence that Trump's public "de-nuclearization" push is cover for an actual re-nuclearization proceeding beneath the surface, with Trump, [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] and [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] running parallel pathways toward acceleration.[215]

Trump also signed an executive order downgrading cannabis from Schedule I to Schedule III, easing restrictions and allowing more research while stopping short of legalization or decriminalization, and authorizing a pilot program to reimburse Medicare patients for CBD products.[215] Announcing it from the Oval Office, he said, "We have people begging for me to do this, people that are in great pain for decades," while stressing that the order "doesn't legalize marijuana in any way, shape or form."[215]

On foreign policy, US officials cited by Politico and The Times described the [[european-union|EU]]'s insistence on seizing frozen Russian central bank assets as an obstacle to Trump's attempts to resolve the Ukraine conflict, with Washington backchanneling with European capitals as Italy, Bulgaria, Malta and the Czech Republic joined the states opposing the seizure; Trump has proposed that the assets be unfrozen and invested in US-led reconstruction in Ukraine and joint projects with Moscow.[215]
## 2024 Time Person of the Year

On December 12, 2024, Trump was named [[time-person-of-the-year|Time's 2024 Person of the Year]] in recognition of what the magazine called a comeback of historic proportions during a year in which he weathered assassination attempts and felony convictions.[216] Time credited him with "driving a once-in-a-generation political realignment" and "reshaping the American presidency and altering America's role in the world," and he rang the opening bell at the New York Stock Exchange to mark the occasion.[216] He had also been named Person of the Year in 2016.[216]

In the accompanying interview he claimed the Biden administration had lost track of more than 300,000 [[unaccompanied-migrant-children|unaccompanied migrant children]], and, asked whether he trusted [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]], answered, "I don't trust anybody."[216] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the remark as an assertion that Trump distrusts the other players because he knows the game intimately, concluding that "they're trapped in the story with him."[216]

## Transition-period developments (December 2024)

[[meta-platforms|Meta]] donated $1 million to Trump's inaugural fund in December 2024, two weeks after [[mark-zuckerberg|Mark Zuckerberg]] dined with him at Mar-a-Lago — a reversal after Meta had banned Trump following January 6 and after Trump wrote that Zuckerberg would "spend the rest of his life in prison" if he tried to influence the election.[216] See [[trump-inaugural-fund-donations|Trump inaugural fund donations]].[216]

Trump also invited Chinese President [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] to attend his January inauguration, a move former House Speaker [[newt-gingrich|Newt Gingrich]] called "tremendous" and estimated at a 50/50 chance of acceptance, saying Xi's presence would be a "signal to the planet."[216] The transition team floated other leaders including Hungarian Prime Minister Viktor Orbán, even as Trump prepared 10 percent tariffs on all Chinese goods plus levies on Canada and Mexico for his first day in office.[216]

Politico reported that Trump's mixed messaging left Democrats struggling to respond: he expressed interest in a deal to keep Dreamers in the country while vowing to end birthright citizenship, pledged to work with Democratic mayors and governors on crime in the same interview in which he suggested lawmakers who investigated him over the Capitol riot should go to jail, and repeatedly said he was unlikely to restrict access to medication abortion.[216] Minnesota House Speaker Melissa Hortman said, "Right now, we're still sorting out what he's actually serious about."[216]

## Commentary on the Menendez indictment double standard (September 2023)

Following Senator [[robert-menendez|Robert Menendez]]'s defiant September 2023 refusal to resign over federal bribery charges, Trump used Truth Social to call out what Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] described as a two-tiered standard, in which allegations are treated as convictions for Trump and other anti-communist figures but as mere accusations for Democratic officials.[217] She noted the post was circulating on every social platform, summarizing the moment as "it's raining red pills."[217] The same brief recorded that Trump was polling higher than he ever had, which [[paul-fleuret|Paul Fleuret]] argued guaranteed escalating Russian-interference rhetoric from the [[hillary-clinton|Clinton]] camp as November 2024 approached.[217]

## July 2025: Epstein backlash, Fed threats, and NATO arms gambit

In mid-July 2025 Trump called his own supporters gullible "weaklings" for questioning the transparency of a secretive government inquiry into [[jeffrey-epstein|Jeffrey Epstein]], spending another day trying to stamp out a conspiracy-fueled uproar among his MAGA base.[218] The same week he privately indicated he was on the verge of firing [[federal-reserve|Federal Reserve]] chair [[jerome-powell|Jerome Powell]], rattling Wall Street and renewing questions over the central bank's independence.[218]

On July 16, 2025 Trump embraced the FBI's decision to open a conspiracy probe into a decade of alleged intelligence abuses and weaponized law enforcement, suggesting it be led by a special prosecutor who could also examine "credible evidence" in the Epstein case, and vowing to declassify two highly sensitive pieces of intelligence.[218] "It was a disgrace what happened, what happened in 2016 and what happened in 2020," he said, adding that "our voting has to be straightened out."[218] See [[grand-conspiracy-probe|grand conspiracy probe]].[218] His [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] simultaneously sought voter registration lists and election records from at least nine states ahead of the [[2026-midterm-elections|2026 midterms]], following his March directive to Attorney General [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]].[218]

In foreign policy, Trump announced a scheme under which the United States would manufacture weapons for [[ukraine|Ukraine]] to be paid for by European [[nato|NATO]] members - "We're not buying it. We will manufacture it, and they're going to be paying for it" - a plan several member states said they learned of only from the press conference with [[mark-rutte|Mark Rutte]].[218] His statement that Kiev would receive "17 Patriots" left Ukrainian commanders unable to determine whether he meant launchers or full [[patriot-missile-system|Patriot]] batteries.[218] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] dubbed him "the Chaos Conductor," reading the ambiguity and the funding demand as a deliberate effort to bankrupt and expose NATO's European members.[218]

## Post-conviction polling (June 2024)

An ABC News/Ipsos poll published in early June 2024 found 47% of Americans believed the [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]] prosecution of Trump was politically motivated against 38% who did not, while 50% considered the guilty verdict correct.[219] Trump's favorability was effectively flat after the verdict at 31%, compared with 29% in March 2024.[219] Badlands contributor [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] argued that support for Trump had risen across demographics following the 34-count conviction and that the poll was an attempt to portray that surge as a [[red-mirage|red mirage]].[219]

In the same period, Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] publicly defended Trump, telling international journalists that "the ex-president is being persecuted for political reasons, and this fact destroys Washington's imaginary leadership in the sphere of democracy and even the country's political system."[219] Separately, [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]]'s [[x-corp|X Corp.]] asked the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] to bar secret government demands for platform user data of the kind special counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] had used to obtain Trump's account records, and Musk denied reports that he was in line for an advisory role in a second Trump administration.[219]

## July 2024: immunity ruling fallout and NATO posture

On July 2, 2024, sentencing in Trump's New York [[trump-hush-money-case|hush money case]] was postponed by Justice [[juan-merchan|Juan Merchan]] until at least September 18, as the judge weighed the Supreme Court's ruling on [[presidential-immunity|presidential immunity]]; Trump's lawyers argued the ruling merited tossing out the conviction entirely, and he denied any wrongdoing.[220] Writing in all capital letters on [[truth-social|Truth Social]], Trump said "the impact of the Immunity Ruling is a loud and clear signal for Justice in the United States" and claimed it delivered "total exoneration" in this and other criminal cases.[220] The same week his former attorney [[rudy-giuliani|Rudy Giuliani]] was disbarred in New York over false claims about the 2020 election.[220]

On foreign policy, POLITICO reported that despite years of threats Trump would be unlikely to quit [[nato|NATO]] outright in a second term, according to former Trump national security officials and defense experts expected to serve again — but that the alliance would not survive intact either.[220] In exchange for continued U.S. participation he would demand drastically higher European spending and what defense expert [[dan-caldwell|Dan Caldwell]] called a "radical reorientation" of NATO, with Caldwell citing rising U.S. debt, flagging recruiting and an overstretched defense industrial base.[220] Trump's June 27 debate performance against President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] also continued to reverberate; of one of Biden's answers on immigration Trump said, "I really don't know what he said at the end of that sentence. I don't think he knows what he said either."[220]

## September 2024 campaign and diplomacy

On September 23, 2024, Trump met Qatari Emir [[tamim-bin-hamad-al-thani|Tamim Al Thani]] and Prime Minister [[mohammed-bin-abdulrahman-al-thani|Mohammed bin Abdulrahman Al Thani]] at his Mar-a-Lago resort in Florida, praising [[qatar|Qatar]]'s leadership on Truth Social as advancing "on all levels at record speed" and as strongly wanting "peace in the Middle East and all over the world."[221] Badlands Media noted the timing alongside [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s declaration of war against [[hezbollah|Hezbollah]], observing that Trump was rekindling ties with the nation Netanyahu has long accused of financing [[hamas|Hamas]], and concluding that Trump, "master of deception," was "throwing smoke in every direction."[221]

In an hour-long phone interview with [[breitbart|Breitbart News]], Trump previewed a Pennsylvania speech targeting Chinese Communist Party purchases of American farmland: "We have to have control of our land. We have to have control of our economy. We have to have control of our borders. We have to make sure we're in charge of our own country."[221] He argued the United States was being "sold out from under us" and asked whether China would permit reciprocal purchases of its land.[221] Badlands framed reclaiming American farmland as central to making America healthy and great again, contrasting Trump's approach with celebrity philanthropy such as [[farm-aid|Farm Aid]].[221]

Badlands also cited the endorsement of Trump by Hamtramck, Michigan mayor [[amer-ghalib|Amer Ghalib]] and the [[teamsters|Teamsters]]' refusal to endorse [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] as evidence of gains "across all domains and demographics."[221] Separately, The Guardian reported that Democrats were increasingly worried pollsters were undercounting Trump's support, particularly in Pennsylvania, Michigan and Wisconsin, recalling that he outperformed forecasts in all three states in 2016 and 2020.[221]

## August 2024 campaign developments

Trump's live X "Spaces" interview with [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] in August 2024 was disrupted by technical failures that the pro-Palestinian group [[rippersec|RipperSec]] claimed as a cyber-attack, with a cybersecurity firm describing a "highly targeted operation, specifically aimed at Donald Trump's personal Twitter [X] account."[220] [[google-threat-analysis-group|Google's Threat Analysis Group]] separately reported Iranian phishing attempts against personal email accounts of individuals affiliated with both Trump and President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], lending credence to the Trump campaign's claim that it had been hacked by an [[iran|Iranian]] group.[220]

As Trump fell behind Vice President [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] in fundraising and some swing-state polling, his campaign expanded its senior ranks, bringing back 2016 campaign manager [[corey-lewandowski|Corey Lewandowski]] and several other former aides in advisory roles.[220] Trump had by then challenged Harris to three debates, and his running mate [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] accepted vice presidential debates on both October 1 and September 18.[220] Independent candidate [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]], who had met Trump in Milwaukee the previous month to discuss a policy role and endorsement without reaching agreement, was simultaneously approaching the Harris camp about a Cabinet position.[220]

Badlands commentary highlighted a December 2021 interview, recorded by the Israeli press, in which Trump drew a sharp contrast between [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] and [[mahmoud-abbas|Mahmoud Abbas]] and said he believed Netanyahu never wanted peace with Palestine, arguing that Trump, [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and Saudi Crown Prince Mohammed bin Salman were converging on a "Sovereign Alliance" to end the globalist war machine.[220]

## February 2024: attrition, courts and NATO remarks

In mid-February 2024, Trump's legal fortunes improved markedly. Legal scholar [[jonathan-turley|Jonathan Turley]] wrote that Trump had had one of his best weeks as cases and critics imploded — the ballot disqualification effort in Washington and the prosecutorial scandal in Georgia alike — and argued Trump might win "by simple attrition rather than vindication," though he remained in a war of attrition and was "not out of the woods."[222] The [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] gave the Fourteenth Amendment disqualification theory championed by [[laurence-tribe|Laurence Tribe]] a reception Turley called glacial, with even [[ketanji-brown-jackson|Ketanji Brown Jackson]] calling the effort anti-democratic, while the [[state-of-georgia-v-trump|Georgia case]] floundered amid allegations against [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]] and [[nathan-wade|Nathan Wade]], and calls mounted in New York for the removal of [[alvin-bragg|Alvin Bragg]].[222]

On Monday, February 12, 2024, Trump attended federal court in Florida, meeting Judge [[aileen-cannon|Aileen Cannon]] face-to-face for the first time in the [[trump-classified-documents-case|classified documents case]]; he arrived by motorcade at about 9 a.m., stayed just over five hours through two closed-door sessions on classified evidence, and left without speaking to the press as supporters played "God Bless the USA."[222]

Polling that week found 62% of Americans considered Trump, then 77, too old to serve as president — including 71% of independents but only 35% of Republicans — against 86% who said the same of [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]].[222] [[simon-esler|Simon Esler]] suggested the precedent of removing Biden for age could be mirrored onto Trump, and read Trump's cultivated public closeness with [[vivek-ramaswamy|Vivek Ramaswamy]], the youngest major Republican candidate in history, as a counter-move injecting youthful leadership into his campaign.[222]

Over the preceding weekend Trump had said at a rally that he would be willing to let Russia "do whatever the hell they want" to [[nato|NATO]] allies that do not contribute sufficiently to collective defense.[222] The New York Times reported that the remark stunned many in Europe and threatened to push a previously quiet debate about preparing for a world without American leadership of the 75-year-old alliance into a far more public phase, with the larger implication that Trump might invite [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] to pick off a NATO nation as a lesson to the others.[222] White House spokesman [[andrew-bates|Andrew Bates]] denounced the comments as "unhinged," though by Sunday they had resonated with those arguing Europe cannot depend on the United States to deter Russia.[222]

## September 2025: Kirk assassination, tariff pressure, and the 9/11 anniversary

Following the [[assassination-of-charlie-kirk|assassination of Charlie Kirk]], Trump blamed the left and vowed vengeance while no shooter had yet been identified.[223] Trump also asked the [[european-union|European Union]] to impose tariffs of up to 100% on China and India over their purchases of Russian oil, a request first reported by the Financial Times and confirmed to CNBC by two sources; the US was reportedly prepared to "mirror" any tariffs Europe imposed, though Europe was seen as unlikely to acquiesce.[223] Separately, Trump had threatened to withhold federal funding for the rebuild of Baltimore's [[francis-scott-key-bridge-collapse|Francis Scott Key Bridge]] after a social media quarrel with Maryland Governor [[wes-moore|Wes Moore]] over Baltimore crime, writing on Truth Social: "I gave Wes Moore a lot of money to fix his demolished bridge. I will now have to rethink that decision???"[223]

On September 11, 2025, Trump attended a [[new-york-yankees|New York Yankees]] game in the Bronx on the anniversary of the [[september-11-attacks|September 11 attacks]], drawing a mix of cheers and jeers; he sat in a private box with team president [[randy-levine|Randy Levine]] and, before the game, greeted [[aaron-judge|Aaron Judge]], manager [[aaron-boone|Aaron Boone]] and others in the clubhouse, recalling his friendship with the late owner [[george-steinbrenner|George Steinbrenner]].[223] His administration's immigration crackdown also drew scrutiny that week after [[immigration-and-customs-enforcement|ICE]] detained a long-resident Los Angeles street vendor holding a green card.[223]


## October 2025: dealmaking with Xi and Putin

Bloomberg reported in October 2025 that a single throughline ran through Trump's approach to his biggest foreign policy challenges: "Let's make a deal."[224] Asked whether his latest tariff broadside against Beijing was more negotiating ploy than firm policy, Trump said of his threatened 145% tariffs on Chinese goods, "It's not sustainable," adding in a Fox Business interview, "It could stand, but they forced me to do that."[224] He also dialed back a veiled Truth Social threat to cancel a meeting with [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]] at a gathering of Asia-Pacific leaders later that month, saying during a White House meeting with Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] that "we'd like to meet" — remarks that soothed a Wall Street rattled by fears of a renewed tit-for-tat trade war.[224] His appetite for dealmaking was also expected to take him to Budapest for a meeting with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] in the following weeks.[224]

[[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read the pattern as evidence that Trump and Xi are waging economic war not against each other but against the [[deep-state|Deep State]], running a playbook arguably authored by Putin during his subordination of the [[boris-yeltsin|Yeltsin]]-era oligarchy — a program the writer calls the [[good-reset|Good Reset]] and links to the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]].[224]

Separately, the same brief covered Trump's commutation of former Rep. [[george-santos|George Santos]]'s seven-year sentence, defended by Speaker [[mike-johnson|Mike Johnson]] as a constitutional exercise of clemency grounded in belief in redemption; the [[white-house|White House]]'s arrival on [[bluesky|Bluesky]], where it became the second most-blocked account behind Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]]; the administration's race to respond to [[china-rare-earth-export-restrictions-2025|Chinese rare earth export restrictions]]; and cuts to global health funding that Politico reported had reached a 15-year low in 2025.[224] Badlands commentary on the second round of the [[no-kings-protests|No Kings protests]] noted that if Trump were the dictator his critics allege, such rallies would not be permitted at all.[224]

## Agenda 47 announcement (March 2023)

In early March 2023 Trump announced his [[agenda-47|Agenda 47]] initiatives, vowing to give American families and individuals "a new shot at the American Dream" and promising "a quantum leap in the American standard of living."[226] The plan encompassed building new cities, a transportation revolution, returning manufacturing to the United States, and lowering the cost of living while rebuilding and beautifying American cities, which Trump framed as a "new Quantum Leap plan that will create a new American future and modernize communities across the country."[226] Commentators presented it as a rejoinder to the [[world-economic-forum|World Economic Forum]]'s [[agenda-2030|Agenda 2030]], and Badlands contributor [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] described the four-minute rollout video as Trump's first concrete set of solutions and "an all-out assault on the globalist agenda."[226]

## Economic Club of New York address, September 2024

Speaking at the Economic Club of New York on September 5, 2024, Trump unveiled a slate of economic proposals headlined by a [[government-efficiency-commission|government efficiency commission]] that he said [[elon-musk|Elon Musk]] had agreed to lead if he were elected in November, tasked with a complete financial and performance audit of the federal government and with recommending drastic reforms.[227] He credited the idea to Musk, who had suggested it during a conversation on X and to whom Trump had replied "I'd love it," and who had given him his "complete and total endorsement."[227] The speech also proposed further cuts to the corporate tax rate, deregulation including of energy production, rescinding all unspent funds from a key law passed under the Biden administration, an embrace of cryptocurrency, and sweeping tariffs on imports, days before his scheduled debate with [[kamala-harris|Kamala Harris]] in Pennsylvania.[227]

The same week, former Republican Rep. [[liz-cheney|Liz Cheney]] announced she would vote for Harris, citing "the danger that Donald Trump poses," while [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] joked at the [[eastern-economic-forum|Eastern Economic Forum]] that Russia would back Harris.[227] Badlands writer [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] noted that Putin's rhetoric on the Biden-to-Harris switch nearly mirrored Trump's own remark that Harris "would be my second choice" as candidate, reading the parallel as evidence the two were "on the same side, fighting against the same people."[227]

## Crypto donations and the 2024 campaign

In May 2024 Trump's campaign announced it would begin accepting cryptocurrency donations, casting the move as solidarity with opponents of "socialistic government control" over U.S. financial markets; supporters could donate using any cryptocurrency accepted through the [[coinbase|Coinbase]] Commerce product.[228] The announcement tied President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] to Senator [[elizabeth-warren|Elizabeth Warren]], a vocal crypto critic who had posted a March 2023 advertisement declaring she was building an "anti-crypto army," and the campaign responded that "MAGA supporters, now with a new cryptocurrency option, will build a crypto army moving the campaign to victory on November 5th!"[228]

Days later the Biden White House issued a statement of administration policy opposing passage of the [[financial-innovation-and-technology-for-the-21st-century-act|Financial Innovation and Technology for the 21st Century Act]] without threatening a veto, which Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] read as evidence that Trump puppeteers not only the [[media-industrial-complex|Media Industrial Complex]] but the Biden administration itself, forcing capitulation days after it had threatened to bring the hammer down on the industry.[228]

## Other 2024 items

Trump's endorsement of [[vince-fong|Vince Fong]] in the May 2024 California special election to complete [[kevin-mccarthy|Kevin McCarthy]]'s term was watched as a proxy measure of the former president's clout ahead of his all-but-certain November matchup with Biden; Fong defeated Tulare County Sheriff [[mike-boudreaux|Mike Boudreaux]].[228] The same brief marked the seven-year anniversary of the [[sword-dance|Sword Dance]] hosted for Trump by [[king-salman|King Salman]] of [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]], and recalled Trump's diplomacy with [[kim-jong-un|Kim Jong Un]], whom former South Korean president [[moon-jae-in|Moon Jae-in]] described in a new memoir as willing to forsake nuclear weapons in exchange for a guarantee of regime survival.[228]

## Dismissal of the federal prosecutions (November 2024)

On 25 November 2024, weeks after Trump's election victory, Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] sought dismissal of both the federal election subversion case and the classified documents case against the president-elect, writing that the Constitution required dismissal before inauguration and that the outcome was "not based on the merits or strength of the case."[229] Judge [[tanya-chutkan|Tanya Chutkan]] dismissed the election case without prejudice that afternoon.[229] Trump had said he would fire Smith on retaking office; the two-year pursuit had marked the first time a former occupant of the White House faced federal criminal charges, and although the Supreme Court's landmark summer 2024 immunity ruling narrowed the case, Trump's strategy of delay ensured no trial began before the election.[229] [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued that the "Witch Hunt" had only bolstered Trump's campaign, and had in fact functioned as the campaign.[229]

## Transition and cabinet formation

A CBS/YouGov poll released on 25 November 2024 found that 59% of Americans approved of Trump's handling of his transition and 55% felt happy or satisfied with his victory, while 95% of Republicans but only 15% of Democrats felt optimistic or excited; only 44% of Democrats said they were motivated to oppose him.[229] [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] read the numbers as evidence of Democratic exhaustion and of a party discovering it was a minority, and dismissed the "not a landslide" message as an effort by party bosses to temper Trump's mandate.[229] Trump's picks came at a rapid clip, often announced over social media; after [[matt-gaetz|Matt Gaetz]]'s withdrawal he named [[pam-bondi|Pam Bondi]] to the Justice Department, and was reported to be preparing a senior [[fbi|FBI]] or DOJ role for [[kash-patel|Kash Patel]].[229] The transition's failure to sign the standard ethics and transparency agreements barred its landing teams from federal agencies, denied it cybersecurity support, secure email and FBI background checks, and left [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy Jr.]]'s advisers rebuffed by the [[department-of-health-and-human-services|Department of Health and Human Services]].[229]

## Day-one energy plan

Reuters reported that the transition was assembling a wide-ranging energy package for rollout within days of inauguration that would approve export permits for new liquefied natural gas projects, expand oil drilling offshore and on federal lands, repeal Biden-era climate measures including electric vehicle tax credits and clean power plant standards, lift the pause on LNG export permits, reopen five-year offshore drilling plans to more lease sales, and symbolically approve the [[keystone-pipeline|Keystone Pipeline]].[229] Trump also promised to declare an energy emergency on his first day to test whether he could bypass congressional and regulatory barriers.[229]

## October 2025 news cycle

On October 6, 2025, President Donald Trump said he was "pretty sure" there would be a deal ending the Gaza war, while denying reports that he had privately told Israeli Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] to stop being so "f*cking negative" and to "take the win" after [[hamas|Hamas]] accepted parts of Washington's proposal.[230] "No, it's not true. He's been very positive on the deal," Trump said of Netanyahu, and told reporters in the Oval Office that he did have red lines for Hamas in the fresh round of negotiations that opened that day in Egypt: "If certain things aren't met, we're not going to do it."[230] Commentary in the [[badlands-news-brief|Badlands News Brief]] argued that Trump was "twisting the knife" on Netanyahu — damaging his political clout privately while maintaining positive public optics — leaving the Israeli premier unable to correct the record without further weakening himself.[230]

On the same day, Trump left open the possibility of a pardon for [[ghislaine-maxwell|Ghislaine Maxwell]], saying he would speak to the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]] about it, and disclosed that [[sean-combs|Sean "Diddy" Combs]] had requested a pardon after being sentenced to more than four years in prison.[230] Trump also directed a review of federal funding to Portland, Oregon, and his administration appealed a judicial order blocking [[national-guard|National Guard]] deployments there.[230] Badlands commentators additionally noted that Trump had threatened [[george-soros|George Soros]] with RICO charges during the summer of 2025, while his Treasury Secretary [[scott-bessent|Scott Bessent]] was a Soros Fund Management alumnus.[230]

## "Violent day" of policing remark (September 2024)

At a rally in Erie, Pennsylvania on Sunday, September 29, 2024 — thirty-six days before the election — Trump called for "one real rough, nasty" and "violent day" of police retaliation as a means of eradicating crime "immediately."[231] "One rough hour — and I mean real rough — the word will get out and it will end immediately, you know? It will end immediately," he said.[231]

A Trump campaign official said the remarks did not amount to a new policy proposal and that the candidate was "clearly just floating it in jest."[231] Communications director [[steven-cheung]] added that "President Trump has always been the law and order President and he continues to reiterate the importance of enforcing existing laws," contrasting this with what he called the "all-out anarchy" created by [[kamala-harris]] in some American communities, particularly during her tenure as California Attorney General when, in his telling, she emboldened criminals.[231]

### February 2025: Saudi investment summit, Iron Dome, and DOGE dividend
On February 19, 2025, Trump became the first sitting American president to address the Future Investment Initiative Institute, speaking at the Faena Forum on Miami Beach to open the FII Priority Summit Miami 2025.[232] The event was backed by Saudi Arabia's sovereign wealth fund, which Bloomberg estimated held $925 billion in assets under management, and Trump used the platform to press the oil-rich kingdom to invest $1 trillion in the United States.[232] His Doral resort was scheduled to host the Saudi-backed LIV Golf tournament in April, and the audience included Elon Musk alongside Trump's chief of staff and national security adviser.[232] Badlands commentary framed the speech as Trump paying reverence to Crown Prince Mohammed bin Salman and the emerging multipolar world order, casting Saudi Arabia as "one of our greatest allies of the Golden Age."[232]

At the same Miami Beach speech, Trump floated a "DOGE dividend," saying that "there's even under consideration a new concept where we give 20% of the DOGE savings to American citizens and 20% goes to paying down debt."[232] The idea followed a post by investor James Fishback and a Musk reply that he would "check with the President."[232]

The previous month Trump had directed the construction of an American "Iron Dome" missile defense shield, writing in an executive order that "over the past 40 years, rather than lessening, the threat from next-generation strategic weapons has become more intense and complex with the development by peer and near-peer adversaries of next-generation delivery systems."[232] The White House said the plan would "further the goal of peace through strength," though outside experts argued the concept defied physics and practicality.[232]

Trump also drew attention for claiming, without citing evidence, that Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelensky's approval rating was "down at four percent," and for calling on Ukraine to hold elections despite martial law barring them.[232] A Kyiv International Institute of Sociology poll released the following day put trust in Zelensky at 57 percent.[232] Separately, Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick said Trump's goal was to abolish the Internal Revenue Service in favor of an "External Revenue Service" funded by tariffs and foreign revenue.[232]

## Chronic childhood illness pledge (June 2023)

In a June 2023 [[agenda-47|Agenda 47]] campaign video, Trump promised to address the sharp rise in the nation's chronic illnesses and health problems, particularly among children, as part of his plans for a return to the Oval Office.[233] He said he would establish a [[chronic-childhood-illness-commission|special presidential commission]] "of independent minds who are not bought and paid for by [[big-pharma|Big Pharma]]" and charge it with investigating what is causing the decades-long increase in chronic illnesses and with publishing recommendations for how "every American child can have a safe and healthy childhood."[233] "I understand Big Pharma, I believe better than anyone else," he added; "I know where they're coming from."[233] Badlands contributor [[justin-deschamps|Justin Deschamps]] read the pledge as part of Trump's broader dismantling of what he called the globalist plantation.[233]


## April 2025 tariffs, elections order, and third-term talk

Speaking to reporters on Thursday, April 3, 2025, as markets absorbed his [[liberation-day-tariffs|"Liberation Day" tariffs]], Trump said his new tariff plan was "going very well" and predicted: "The markets are going to boom. The stock is going to boom. The country is going to boom."[234] The [[white-house|White House]] sent conflicting messages about whether the duties were permanent or a negotiating tactic, with Trump saying they "give us great power to negotiate."[234] Badlands writers cast the resulting turmoil as intentional: [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] argued the administration was leaning into the economic panic pattern because it knew how the story ended on market fundamentals and wanted the system to go "all-in against the solution," while [[ashe-in-america|Ashe in America]] framed the sell-off as the visible cost of reclaiming power from global corporations, noting the effort was only in "Week 11."[234]

The same week, Democratic officials in 19 states sued over Trump's [[2025-trump-elections-executive-order|elections executive order]], the fourth suit against a directive issued a week earlier that would require documentary proof of citizenship to register to vote and demand that mail ballots be received by Election Day.[234] Trump's order asserted that the United States had failed "to enforce basic and necessary election protection," while state attorneys general countered that "the President has no power to do any of this."[234]

Trump also revived speculation about a third term, telling journalists that "people" were asking him to run again and saying, "I don't know, I never looked into it. They do say there is a way you can do it," and telling NBC News by phone, "I like working, I'm not joking," despite the [[twenty-second-amendment-third-term-loophole|Twenty-Second Amendment]]'s two-election limit.[234] Burning Bright urged readers not to dismiss what he called Trump's "latest memetic deployment" about a fourth term, and to read it in light of [[devolution|Devolution]] theory.[234]

## Return to Capitol Hill and the all-tariff proposal (June 2024)

On Thursday, June 13, 2024, Trump returned to Capitol Hill to meet congressional Republicans for the first time since the January 6, 2021 attack, arriving as the party's presumptive nominee despite pending federal charges over the 2020 election and a recent hush-money conviction.[235] House Republicans sang "Happy Birthday" to him at a private breakfast and presented a baseball and bat from the congressional game, while senators produced an American flag cake with "45" candles and then "47"; Trump boasted his telephone rallies drew bigger crowds than [[taylor-swift|Taylor Swift]].[235] In a separate meeting the same day at the Capitol Hill Club he floated an "all tariff policy" that would let the United States abolish the [[federal-income-tax|federal income tax]], and discussed using tariffs as negotiating leverage.[235] See [[2024-trump-return-to-capitol-hill]] and [[trump-all-tariff-policy-proposal]].

Two days earlier, on June 11, Trump had met at Mar-a-Lago with executives representing roughly 30% of the [[bitcoin]] network's mining capacity, who described him as prepared and informed and pleased by an American-made Auradine ASIC chip.[235] On Ukraine, he had declined to say whether he would continue US support if elected, offering only that he would negotiate a quick end to the war, and had said he would "encourage" Russia "to do whatever the hell they want" if European states did not increase defense spending.[235]

## Thanksgiving 2025 statements

Over the 2025 Thanksgiving holiday, Trump issued a series of statements that dominated the news cycle. In a lengthy Truth Social post he denounced decades of immigration policy, asserting that the official U.S. foreign-born population stood at 53 million people according to the Census and that most were on welfare or came from failed nations, prisons, mental institutions, gangs or drug cartels.[236] He claimed a migrant earning $30,000 with a green card would receive roughly $50,000 in yearly family benefits, and described the refugee burden as the leading cause of social dysfunction in America — citing failed schools, high crime, urban decay, overcrowded hospitals, housing shortages and large deficits.[236] He singled out Somali resettlement in Minnesota, called Governor [[tim-walz|Tim Walz]] "seriously retarded" for inaction, and attacked Representative [[ilhan-omar|Ilhan Omar]].[236]

In the same statement Trump announced that he would permanently pause migration from all Third World countries, terminate the millions of Biden-era admissions including those signed by autopen, end federal benefits and subsidies to noncitizens, denaturalize migrants who undermine domestic tranquility, and deport foreign nationals who are public charges, security risks, or incompatible with Western civilization — concluding that "only REVERSE MIGRATION can fully cure this situation."[236]

He separately celebrated the dismissal of the Georgia RICO case brought by [[fani-willis|Fani Willis]], calling it a corrupt witch hunt orchestrated with [[nathan-wade|Nathan Wade]] at the direction of [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]], and predicted the remaining prosecutions would end the same way.[236]

## Ukraine peace framework and Saudi normalization (November 2025)

Trump's administration circulated a 28-point plan to end the war in [[ukraine|Ukraine]] that alarmed Kyiv and European allies for heavily favoring Moscow, prompting EU foreign policy chief [[kaja-kallas|Kaja Kallas]] to insist that [[russia|Russia]] must make concrete concessions.[236] Trump publicly maintained that peace would come only at the "final stages," a formulation echoed by [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and read by [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] as evidence that the underlying deals were already settled.[236] His envoy [[steve-witkoff|Steve Witkoff]] continued shuttling to Moscow amid a leaked-transcript controversy.[236]

Israeli television reported that Trump was left "disappointed and angry" after a tense White House exchange with Saudi Crown Prince [[mohammed-bin-salman|Mohammed bin Salman]], in which Trump urged immediate normalization with [[israel|Israel]] and the crown prince demurred, citing anti-Israel sentiment in Saudi Arabia after the Gaza war.[236] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] doubted the account, arguing Trump knew in advance that bin Salman would not move without progress on Palestinian statehood — a pathway Trump himself had built into his 20-point peace plan.[236]

## July 2024 campaign strategy and challenges to Biden

In July 2024 advisers told [[axios|Axios]] that Trump was adjusting his agenda, the Republican platform, his vice-presidential plans and even his debate style to win over more than a half-dozen persuadable voter groups across seven states.[237] Every move — from personally editing the party platform to lying low while [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s debate debacle absorbed attention — was described as designed to nudge "double-haters" and genuinely undecided voters.[237] The campaign ranked the swing states from hardest to easiest as Pennsylvania, Wisconsin, Michigan, Georgia, North Carolina, Nevada and Arizona, placing Pennsylvania first as the biggest swing state and the one Biden most needed; the running mate's first job, besides fundraising, would be to park there.[237] The Rust Belt appeal of Sen. [[jd-vance|J.D. Vance]] helped propel him into the finalist group for the ticket.[237] The Trump-orchestrated platform adopted that week by the Republican National Committee targeted Rust Belt working-class white voters, security-focused mothers concerned about the border and crime, Hispanic working-class men, and Nevada bartenders angered by taxes on tips.[237]

At a rally in Doral, Florida on Tuesday, July 9, Trump challenged Biden to another debate that week and to a golf match, offering to donate $1 million to "any charity" if Biden won.[237] "Tonight, I'm officially offering Joe the chance to redeem himself in front of the entire world," he told the crowd, proposing a contest "man to man, no moderators."[237] Biden had said during the June debate that he would accept a golf challenge if Trump carried his own bag, but a late-Tuesday statement side-stepped the golf challenge and ignored the debate challenge, mentioning [[project-2025|Project 2025]] instead.[237]

Trump met Hungarian Prime Minister [[viktor-orban|Viktor Orbán]] at Mar-a-Lago on Thursday, July 11, days after Orbán's meetings with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and [[xi-jinping|Xi Jinping]], prompting speculation that the Hungarian leader was acting as an intermediary; Trump had said he believed he could persuade Putin to end the war in Ukraine and release detained Americans if returned to office.[237] Separately, senior U.S. intelligence officials said that week that the Russian government had launched a "whole-of-government" effort to influence the election, favouring Trump through covert social-media use and online propaganda mirroring the 2016 and 2020 cycles.[237]

## Prime-time address and Hanukkah remarks (December 2025)

On Tuesday, December 16, 2025, Trump announced that he would give an address to the nation live from the [[white-house|White House]] at 9 p.m. ET the following night, without clarifying a topic.[238] "My Fellow Americans: I will be giving an ADDRESS TO THE NATION tomorrow night, LIVE FROM THE WHITE HOUSE, at 9 P.M. EST. I look forward to 'seeing' you then. It has been a great year for our Country, and THE BEST IS YET TO COME!" he wrote on social media.[238] Press secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] said Trump would address "all of his historic accomplishments over the past year, and maybe teasing some policy that will be coming in the new year"; his previous formal address had followed the shooting of two West Virginia National Guard members in the capital in November.[238] Badlands contributor [[jordan-sather|Jordan Sather]] noted the "My Fellow Americans" opening, the "The Best Is Yet To Come" closing and the date of the 17th as anon-coded signals.[238]

At the White House Hanukkah party the same week, Trump told guests that [[us-congress|Congress]] "is becoming antisemitic" as the "Jewish lobby" is no longer the strongest in Washington, initially referring to all of Congress before highlighting the Squad of progressive Democrats.[238] "My father would tell me, the most powerful lobby that there is in this country is the Jewish Lobby. It's the Israeli lobby. It's not that way anymore," he said, adding, "I will always be a friend and a champion to the Jewish people."[238] The crowd was relatively smaller than usual owing to construction of the White House East Room, and Trump again mistakenly claimed to have secured the release of all remaining hostages from Gaza, despite the body of Ran Gvili still being held there and the father of slain hostage [[omer-neutra|Omer Neutra]] having politely corrected him at an earlier reception.[238]

Badlands contributor [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] read the remarks as a shot across the bow rather than capitulation, arguing that Trump was reminding the Israel lobby of its waning influence in the same week [[benjamin-netanyahu|Netanyahu]] and the Zionist diaspora were demanding that governments allow [[mossad|Mossad]] to partner with local law enforcement against alleged antisemites worldwide.[238] He cited [[jonathan-greenblatt|Jonathan Greenblatt]] of the [[anti-defamation-league|ADL]] having said even Jews who criticise Israel would be hunted as antisemites, argued that [[mark-levin|Mark Levin]]'s "first two-time non-consecutive Jewish president" flattery masked genuine hostility, and predicted Trump would slap down Netanyahu at their Mar-a-Lago meeting on December 29.[238] In his reading, Trump had "said the quiet part out loud," affirming allegations that the United States is living under State Capture.[238]

## Venezuela oil blockade (December 2025)

On Tuesday, December 16, 2025, Trump imposed an oil blockade on [[venezuela|Venezuela]], redirecting a large American naval armada in the Caribbean from operations against alleged "narcoterrorists" to intercepting sanctioned tankers entering and leaving the country.[238] Announcing the move on Truth Social, he described it as "a total and complete blockade of all sanctioned oil tankers going into, and out of, Venezuela," and said the country was "completely surrounded by the largest armada ever assembled in the history of South America" until it returns "all of the oil, land, and other assets that they previously stole from us."[238] The announcement followed a rare direct US seizure of a large oil tanker off the Venezuelan coast, and came after Trump branded the government of [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolas Maduro]] a "Foreign Terrorist Organization."[238] Badlands commentary noted that Venezuela controls no US land and that Trump's own July exemption allows [[citgo|Citgo]] to import Venezuelan crude, suggesting the "Hostile Regime" language referred instead to a transnational cartel long embedded in the US government.[238]

## BBC lawsuit and SNAP litigation (December 2025)

Also in mid-December 2025, Trump filed a $10 billion defamation lawsuit against the [[bbc|BBC]] in the Southern District of Florida over a documentary that spliced portions of his January 6, 2021 Ellipse speech.[238] Separately, a federal judge delayed his administration's effort to restrict [[supplemental-nutrition-assistance-program|SNAP]] benefits for migrants, extending the state compliance grace period to April 9, 2026; the underlying eligibility restrictions stem from the [[one-big-beautiful-bill-act|One Big Beautiful Bill Act]] he signed on July 4.[238]

## Gaza peace plan and September 2025 diplomacy

Trump unveiled his 20-point plan for ending the war in [[gaza|Gaza]] at a White House press conference with visiting Prime Minister [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]] on Monday 29 September 2025, declaring Washington "beyond very close" to a deal after Jerusalem accepted the proposal and Arab and Muslim countries committed to disarming [[hamas|Hamas]].[239] "Now it's time for Hamas to accept the terms of the plan that we put forward today," he said in the State Dining Room after the pair met in the Oval Office for over an hour.[239] The plan offered Hamas leaders safe passage out of Gaza in exchange for the release of the 48 remaining hostages within 48 hours of Israel's public acceptance, along with prisoner releases by Israel, amnesty for Hamas members committing to peaceful coexistence, immediate humanitarian aid, and a pledge not to strike [[qatar|Qatar]] again.[239] [[ghostofbasedpatrickhenry|GhostofBasedPatrickHenry]] argued the proposal was explicitly based on the [[new-york-declaration|New York Declaration]] and that Trump humiliated Netanyahu at several points during the press conference.[239]

## Shutdown negotiations, September 2025

Trump and Democrats emerged from a private [[white-house|White House]] meeting on 29 September 2025 without an agreement to keep the federal government open, setting up a wide-scale [[october-2025-government-shutdown|shutdown]] beginning at 12:01 a.m. on 1 October if funding lapsed.[239] House Minority Leader [[hakeem-jeffries|Hakeem Jeffries]] said the discussion with the president and Republican leaders had been "frank and direct" but that "significant and meaningful differences remain."[239] Republicans pushed a stopgap extending current funding to 21 November while Democrats sought health care access and subsidy changes.[239]

## YouTube settlement and family business ties

[[youtube|YouTube]] agreed in late September 2025 to pay $24.5 million to settle Trump's lawsuit over the suspension of his account after the 2021 Capitol attack, with $22 million directed to a trust for the National Mall handling the $200 million [[white-house-ballroom|White House ballroom]] project.[239] In the same week, [[affinity-partners|Affinity Partners]] — founded by Trump's son-in-law [[jared-kushner|Jared Kushner]] — joined [[silver-lake-partners|Silver Lake Partners]] and Saudi Arabia's [[public-investment-fund|Public Investment Fund]] in a $55 billion buyout of [[electronic-arts|Electronic Arts]], a deal whose regulatory review was complicated by Saudi involvement amid an unusually close Saudi-Trump family relationship.[239]

## May 2023 Iowa polling lead

A poll released by [[emerson-college|Emerson College]] on May 25, 2023 showed that neither an abortive rally nor an expanding field of challengers had damaged Trump's standing in Iowa: 62 percent of Iowa Republican voters said they would choose him as the party's nominee, a three-to-one margin over Florida Governor [[ron-desantis|Ron DeSantis]] at 20 percent, with [[mike-pence|Mike Pence]] and [[nikki-haley|Nikki Haley]] tied at 5 percent.[240] Trump was preferred across all demographic groups but especially among voters under 35 (75 percent) and those without a college degree (70 percent), while DeSantis closed the gap among postgraduate-degree holders.[240] In a general-election rematch, Trump led President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] in Iowa 49 percent to 38 percent, with 10 percent choosing a third party and 6 percent undecided.[240]

Commenting in the [[badlands-media|Badlands]] News Brief, [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] treated the result as vindication of his November 2022 assertion that Trump had merely appeared weak while in fact being stronger than ever, having weathered a storm of doom-mongering and retaken the MAGA zeitgeist within a week.[240] He framed the coming period as one in which America First committees would continue putting evidence on the record and scrutiny of election systems would intensify, with the DNC, [[robert-f-kennedy-jr|Robert F. Kennedy, Jr.]], DeSantis and [[kari-lake|Kari Lake]] all shaping the run-up to 2024.[240]


## Opposition to US intervention in Syria (December 2024)

As rebels reached the outskirts of [[damascus|Damascus]] in December 2024, President-elect Trump raged against any US military involvement in Syria in a post on Truth Social, his first detailed public remarks on the situation, while taking a potshot at former President [[barack-obama|Barack Obama]].[241] He noted that Russia, a longtime ally of Syria, appeared incapable of stopping a "literal march through Syria" because it was "so tied up in Ukraine, and with the loss there of over 600,000 soldiers," and argued that the overthrow of [[bashar-al-assad|Bashar al-Assad]] and a Russian withdrawal could ultimately benefit Moscow.[241] "There was never much of a benefit in Syria for Russia, other than to make Obama look really stupid," he wrote, adding: "In any event, Syria is a mess, but is not our friend, and THE UNITED STATES SHOULD HAVE NOTHING TO DO WITH IT. THIS IS NOT OUR FIGHT. LET IT PLAY OUT. DO NOT GET INVOLVED!"[241]

Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] framed the statement as evidence that Trump was playing the international game board without committing to a specific outcome beyond keeping the United States out of war, arguing that the Middle East was the defining impossible task of the era and that Trump had praised the defense of Israel while condemning its leadership and alluded to NATO crossing "red lines" against Russia.[241] The same commentary held that Trump needed to be out of formal power during the Biden term so that regime assets could be removed from the board by other players in the [[sovereign-alliance|Sovereign Alliance]], from the Kabul withdrawal to the Donbas.[241]

## Paris visit and Notre Dame reopening

On the same weekend, Trump visited French President [[emmanuel-macron|Emmanuel Macron]] in Paris, saying the meeting was to discuss the world going "crazy."[241] Greeted by Macron at the Elysee Palace, Trump said the two leaders had "really great success working together on defense and offense" during his first administration, praised their "great relationship" and called the French people "spectacular," describing the trip on Truth Social as "A GREAT HONOR."[241] Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] also joined the meeting, greeting Trump with a handshake; Trump has said he will end the war in Ukraine quickly after being sworn in.[241] Trump attended the reopening of [[notre-dame-cathedral|Notre Dame Cathedral]] later that day, as did First Lady [[jill-biden|Jill Biden]], the White House having said President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] could not attend due to a scheduling conflict.[241]

## Tariff warning and Mexican fentanyl seizure

Days after a Trump tariff warning, Mexican security forces announced on December 4, 2024 the largest fentanyl seizure in that country's history, impounding 1,100 kilograms of the synthetic opioid in Sinaloa.[241]

## June 2023: target notification and declassification letter

On June 7, 2023, federal prosecutors notified Trump that he was a criminal target and likely to be indicted imminently over his handling of classified documents, while the [[justice-department|Justice Department]] declined to delay charges to investigate witness-tampering allegations raised by his legal team.[242] Reporting indicated that an indictment crafted by Special Counsel [[jack-smith|Jack Smith]] could be handed up within the week; see [[2023-trump-classified-documents-indictment|2023 Trump classified documents indictment]].[242]

The same week, a letter signed by Trump and dated January 19, 2021 — his last full day in office — was published by [[dc-draino|DC Draino]] and reported as declassifying the [[crossfire-hurricane|Crossfire Hurricane]] documents, a development [[badlands-media|Badlands Media]] commentary framed as undercutting the premise of the [[mar-a-lago-raid|Mar-a-Lago raid]].[242]

Also in June 2023, Trump invited the [[rushingbrook-childrens-choir|Rushingbrook Children's Choir]] of Greenville, South Carolina to perform at his upcoming South Carolina rally after [[united-states-capitol-police|Capitol Police]] stopped the group from singing the national anthem in the Capitol, calling the prohibition "a shame."[242] Commentary in the same brief described Trump as "the only person speaking sensibly" about the war in Ukraine, namely that it needed to end.[242]

## March 2025: Ukraine aid pause, congressional address, and domestic priorities

On March 3, 2025, Trump teased his forthcoming address to a joint session of Congress on Truth Social, writing, "TOMORROW NIGHT WILL BE BIG. I WILL TELL IT LIKE IT IS!"[243] The theme of the address was "The Renewal of the American Dream."[243]

The same day, a White House official said Trump had paused all U.S. military aid to Ukraine following his clash with Ukrainian President [[volodymyr-zelensky|Volodymyr Zelensky]] at the White House the previous Friday, deepening the fissure between the two one-time allies.[243] Trump had upended U.S. policy on Ukraine and Russia upon taking office in January, adopting a more conciliatory stance toward Moscow, and had criticized Zelensky in the Oval Office for being insufficiently grateful for Washington's backing in the war.[243] "President Trump has been clear that he is focused on peace. We need our partners to be committed to that goal as well. We are pausing and reviewing our aid to ensure that it is contributing to a solution," the official said, speaking on condition of anonymity; the White House offered no immediate comment on the scope or duration of the pause and the [[pentagon|Pentagon]] could provide no further details.[243]

Trump also urged Americans to focus on domestic concerns rather than Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]], writing on Truth Social, "We should spend less time worrying about Putin, and more time worrying about migrant rape gangs, drug lords, murderers, and people from mental institutions entering our Country – So that we don't end up like Europe!"[243] Russian Foreign Minister [[sergey-lavrov|Sergey Lavrov]] commended Trump's approach as one that "largely aligns with our vision."[243]

In other developments that week, the Senate confirmed [[linda-mcmahon|Linda McMahon]] as Secretary of Education by a 51-45 party-line vote, the [[department-of-justice|Justice Department]] announced a review of [[tina-peters|Tina Peters]]'s Colorado election tampering conviction, and freed Gaza hostage [[eli-sharabi|Eli Sharabi]] was reported to be meeting Trump at the White House alongside other released captives.[243]

## December 2025 developments

Trump continued to escalate his pressure campaign on Venezuelan president [[nicolas-maduro|Nicolas Maduro]] in December 2025, announcing a [[2025-venezuela-oil-tanker-blockade|blockade]] of all sanctioned oil tankers entering and leaving [[venezuela|Venezuela]] and reiterating that the United States would keep or sell the oil it had seized off the Venezuelan coast; asked if the goal was to force Maduro out, he said, "I think it'd be smart for him to do that."[244]

On December 23 the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] rebuffed his administration's plan to deploy [[national-guard|National Guard]] troops in Illinois, a rare defeat at a court with a 6-3 conservative majority.[244] A [[cnn|CNN]] poll on the [[2028-united-states-presidential-election|2028 election]] found 3% of Republicans and Republican-leaning independents wanted Trump to run again despite the two-term limit of the 22nd Amendment.[244] Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] credited him with gutting federal [[diversity-equity-and-inclusion|DEI]] programs.[244]

## Bedminster rally after the Miami arraignment (June 2023)

Hours after pleading not guilty in Miami on June 13, 2023 to criminal charges stemming from his hoarding of secret government documents, Trump delivered an angry, campaign-style speech at his Bedminster, New Jersey golf club — one of the very locations at the center of the federal case against him.[245] He portrayed himself as a victim and President [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]] and the government as "thugs," saying, "It's a political persecution like something straight out of a fascist or communist nation."[245] He also repeated a refrain from his time in the [[white-house|White House]], that the authorities were really going after his supporters.[245]

Supporters gathered at Bedminster serenaded him with an early round of "Happy Birthday," and he was described as reserved while recounting the latest witch hunt and arguing that the United States operates a two-tiered justice system.[245] Addressing the crowd, he said, "they're not coming after me, they're coming after you, and I'm just standing in their way and I will never be moving."[245] Commentary in the [[badlands-news-brief|Badlands News Brief]] held that Trump appeared to be gaining support while establishment Republicans who aligned with the [[department-of-justice|Department of Justice]]'s case lost ground, and that the [[presidential-records-act|Presidential Records Act]] rather than the [[espionage-act|Espionage Act]] governed his handling of the records — see [[trump-classified-documents-case|Trump classified documents case]].[245]

## May 2025: Putin call, Gaza pressure, and the "big, beautiful bill"

Trump spoke with Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] for more than two hours on Monday, May 19, 2025, saying the conversation "went very well" and announcing that talks between Moscow and Kiev on the [[russia-ukraine-war|Ukraine war]] would begin "immediately"; Putin called the exchange "very useful."[246] On [[truth-social|Truth Social]] Trump described the tone and spirit as "excellent" and wrote that "Russia and Ukraine will immediately start negotiations toward a Ceasefire and, more importantly, an END to the War," with the conditions to be settled between the two parties.[246] The [[vatican|Vatican]] indicated interest in hosting the negotiations.[246]

The same period brought reporting that Trump's representatives had privately told [[benjamin-netanyahu|Benjamin Netanyahu]]'s government the United States would "abandon" [[israel|Israel]] if it did not end the war in [[gaza|Gaza]], a claim a US official denied.[246] White House Press Secretary [[karoline-leavitt|Karoline Leavitt]] said "the president wants the war in Gaza to end," following the release of Israeli-American soldier Edan Alexander through direct US–[[hamas|Hamas]] talks.[246] During his Middle East tour — which pointedly excluded Netanyahu — Trump met leaders of Saudi Arabia, Qatar and the United Arab Emirates and said of Gaza, "People are starving. Terrible things are happening there."[246]

On May 19 Trump also questioned the timing of [[joe-biden|Joe Biden]]'s Stage 4 prostate cancer diagnosis, suggesting without evidence that it had been concealed: "There are things going on that the public wasn't informed, and I think somebody is going to have to speak to his doctor."[246] The following morning he traveled to Capitol Hill to unify divided House Republicans behind the [[one-big-beautiful-bill-act|"big, beautiful bill"]], having implored holdouts to "STOP TALKING, AND GET IT DONE."[246] Separately, an Atlantic profile of Vice President [[jd-vance|JD Vance]] prompted Badlands contributor [[burning-bright|Burning Bright]] to note that Trump alone among America First figures had not anointed Vance his heir apparent.[246]

## New York hush money trial: defense rests (May 2024)

On Tuesday, 21 May 2024, Trump's lawyers rested their defense in his New York 'hush money' trial without calling him to the witness stand, moving the case toward jury deliberation.[247] Defense attorney [[todd-blanche|Todd Blanche]] told Judge [[juan-merchan|Juan M. Merchan]], 'Your honor, the defense rests,' after testimony from a former federal prosecutor who challenged the credibility of the prosecution's key witness.[247] As the first U.S. president to undergo a criminal trial, Trump declined to answer reporters' questions as he left the courthouse, including questions about his decision not to testify; the Republican presumptive presidential nominee had previously said he intended to take the stand.[247] Jurors were dismissed until 28 May for closing arguments, and after more than four weeks of testimony could begin deliberating on 34 felony counts of falsifying business records as soon as the following week.[247]

## Mid-February 2025 weekly wins and controversies

In mid-February 2025 the [[white-house|White House]] published a list of weekly wins, noting that Trump had brought home an American citizen wrongfully detained in Russia and another detained in Belarus; restored a 25% tariff on steel imports and elevated the aluminum tariff to 25%; unveiled a plan for fair and reciprocal trade; spoken with Russian President [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] and Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy in pursuit of peace; hosted Jordan's King Abdullah II, who announced the kingdom would accept 2,000 sick children from Gaza "as quickly as possible"; and joined Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi to announce new deals on immigration, trade, energy and artificial intelligence.[248] [[jon-herold|Jon Herold]] called it a great list of accomplishments and good material to share with friends and family who are less tuned in.[248]

The same week saw the [[2025-steel-and-aluminum-tariffs|steel and aluminum tariffs]] formalized alongside a reciprocal tariff measure, [[saudi-arabia|Saudi Arabia]]'s push for an [[arab-alternative-to-trump-gaza-plan|Arab alternative to Trump's Gaza plan]], and his administration's first appeal to the [[supreme-court-of-the-united-states|Supreme Court]] over the firing of a top ethics official.[248] On Saturday, February 15, Trump posted "[[he-who-saves-his-country-does-not-violate-any-law|He who saves his Country does not violate any Law]]" to Truth Social and X after courts blocked several of his executive actions, drawing warnings that a constitutional crisis was brewing.[248]

Trump also attended the Daytona 500 on Sunday, arriving aboard Air Force One, which flew over Daytona International Speedway before landing at Daytona International Airport to cheers from the crowd; he became the first sitting president to attend two Daytona 500 races, having previously attended in February 2020.[248]

## February 13, 2025: Putin call, Modi deal, and cabinet confirmations

On Wednesday, February 12, 2025, Trump held a call with [[vladimir-putin|Vladimir Putin]] that CNN described as bringing the Russian leader "in from the cold," as the two hatched plans to end the war in Ukraine and agreed to swap presidential visits.[249] Paired with Defense Secretary [[pete-hegseth|Pete Hegseth]]'s message in Brussels that European allies should "take ownership of conventional security on the continent," the day was cast as a watershed in transatlantic relations that underscored Trump's "America First" ideology and his tendency to treat every alliance as a dollars-and-cents value proposition.[249] Trump invoked the traditional American wariness of foreign entanglements, saying, "We have a little thing called an ocean in between."[249]

The following day Trump announced an energy deal with Indian Prime Minister [[narendra-modi|Narendra Modi]] under which Delhi would import more US oil and gas to shrink the bilateral trade deficit, while his reciprocal tariff order remained in place.[249] At the same press conference announcing reciprocal trade, Trump was asked about New York Mayor [[eric-adams|Eric Adams]] and stated definitively that he had not spoken with the [[justice-department|Justice Department]] about the case at all, after prosecutors including [[danielle-sassoon|Danielle Sassoon]] resigned rather than drop it.[249]
